We can completely reconstruct the pseudotrajectory by considering only the sequence
s_{1},\cdots,

s_{k-m}
and the set of tagged particles
[m]
. This is performed in the following definition.

Definition 3.8

(Second definition of a pseudotrajectory)
.

Fix
m\leq n
, an initial position
Z_{n}
and parameters
(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n-m}
and
\omega\subset[n]
with
|\omega|=m
. In order to construct the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega,(s_{k})_{k})
, we need an auxiliary function
\iota:[0,t]\to\mathbb{N}
, which is increasing, piecewise constant
, and left-continuous function.

At
\tau=0
, we set
{\mathsf{Z}}(0,Z_{n},\omega,(s_{k})_{k}):=Z_{n}
and
\iota(0):=1
.

Suppose that the pseudo trajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}(\cdot,Z_{n},\omega,(s_{k})_{k})
and
\iota(\cdot)
are constructed in the time interval
[0,\tau]
. At time
\tau
particles
i
and
j
meet, i.e.

\left|{\mathsf{x}}_{i}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}(\tau)\right|=\varepsilon,\penalty\ \left({\mathsf{x}}_{i}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}(\tau)\right)\left({\mathsf{v}}_{i}(\tau)-{\mathsf{v}}_{j}(\tau)\right)>0.

If
(i,j)\in\omega^{2}
, the two particles interact and we fix
\underset{\tau^{\prime}\searrow\tau}{\lim}\iota(\tau^{\prime}):=\iota(\tau)
.

Otherwise, we fix
\underset{\tau^{\prime}\searrow\tau}{\lim}\iota(\tau^{\prime}):=\iota(\tau)+1
and we look at
s_{\iota(\tau)}
. If
s_{\iota(\tau)}=1
the two particles interact: as long as
|{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}<\varepsilon
, they follow the dynamic

\left\{\begin{split}\dot{{\mathsf{x}}}_{i}={\mathsf{v}}_{i},&\penalty\ \dot{{\mathsf{v}}}_{i}=\frac{\alpha}{\varepsilon}\nabla\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}}{\varepsilon}\right),\penalty\ \\
\dot{{\mathsf{x}}}_{j}={\mathsf{v}}_{j},&\penalty\ \dot{{\mathsf{v}}}_{j}=\frac{-\alpha}{\varepsilon}\nabla\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}}{\varepsilon}\right).\end{split}\right.

If
s_{\iota(\tau)}=-1
the two particles ignore each other: as long as
|{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}|<\varepsilon
,

\left\{\begin{split}\dot{{\mathsf{x}}}_{i}={\mathsf{v}}_{i},&\penalty\ \dot{{\mathsf{v}}}_{i}=0,\\
\dot{{\mathsf{x}}}_{j}={\mathsf{v}}_{j},&\penalty\ \dot{{\mathsf{v}}}_{j}=0.\end{split}\right.

In both cases, we define
\tau^{+}>\tau
as the first time bigger than
\tau
such that

\left|{\mathsf{x}}_{i}(\tau^{+})-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}(\tau^{+})\right|=\varepsilon,\penalty\ \left({\mathsf{x}}_{i}(\tau^{+})-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}(\tau^{+})\right)\left({\mathsf{v}}_{i}(\tau^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{j}(\tau^{+})\right)<0.

We denote
\mathscr{R}^{t}_{\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}\subset\mathbb{D}^{n}
the set of initial parameters such that the pseudotrajectory has a connected collision graph and has neither multiple encounters
, nor recollision or nor overlaps. Hence, on
\mathscr{R}^{t}_{\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}\subset\mathbb{D}^{n}
, the previous construction has no ambiguity.

We can reconstruct the partition
(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})
for given
(s_{i})_{i\leq n-m}
. We define the graph
G
as a subgraph of the collision graph
\mathscr{G}
by removing the edges of the form
(i,j)_{\tau,-1}
(we keep only the interactions). The cluster
\lambda_{1}
is the union of the connected components in
G
of the particles
[m]
. The
(\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})
are the other connected components.

We have the following equality

(3.8)

\begin{array}[]{r@{}l}\Phi^{0,t}_{m,n}[h_{m}](Z_{n})=\frac{1}{(n-m)!}\sum_{(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-m}s_{i}\penalty\ \mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{t}_{[m],(s_{k})_{k}}}h_{m}({\mathsf{Z}}_{[m]}(\tau,Z_{n},[m],(s_{k})_{k}))\end{array}

We denote

(3.9)

\Phi^{>,t}_{m,n}:=\Phi^{t}_{m,n}-\Phi^{0,t}_{m,n}.

3.4.
Iteration of the pseudotrajectory development

The present section is dedicated to the implementation of the short-time sampling (of step
\delta
).

The construction of Section
3.3
is efficient on a short time interval. To raise a long-time result, we need to iterate these kinds of pseudotrajectory representations and to compute sums of the form

\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\circ\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}),

where
n_{0}\leq n_{1}\leq n_{2}
are three integers.

Remark 3.5
.

In the usual framework, the pseudotrajectories are trees
(see for example
[
BGSRS23b
,
BGSRS24
,
LB25
]
): there are more and more particles as we go backward in time. Hence, the development has naturally a semi-group structure, and it is straightforward to continue the development.

In the present discussion, the pseudotrajectories have a graph structure: particles do not disappear. Hence, we need to work to iterate the process (see Figure
5
)
.

We introduce the
semi-tree
condition in order to recover some semi-group structure.

Definition 3.9

(semi-tree condition)
.

Fix
\omega_{1}\subset\omega_{2}
two finite sets,
t
and
\delta
two positive real numbers such that
K:=\tfrac{t}{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Fix
G
a collision graph such that

E(G)\subset\left\{(i,j)_{(\tau,\sigma)}\Big|i,j\in\omega_{2}\,;\,\sigma\in\{\pm 1\}\,;\,\tau\in(0,t)\right\},

and such that
G
is connected and simply-connected.

We define the decreasing sequence of sets
(\varpi_{k})_{k\in[0,K]}
, where
\varpi_{k}
is the connected components of
\omega_{1}
in the graph of edges

\{(i,j)|\exists(\sigma,\tau)\in\{\pm 1\}\times[k\delta,t]\penalty\ \text{such that}\penalty\ (i,j)_{\tau,\sigma}\in G\}.

The graph
G
verifies the semi-tree condition with respect to
\omega_{1}
if for any edges
(i,j)_{\tau,\sigma}\in G
with
\tau
in
[k\delta,(k+1)\delta[
, one has

(i,j)\in\varpi_{k+1}\times(\varpi_{k}\setminus\varpi_{k+1})\cup(\varpi_{k}\setminus\varpi_{k+1})\times\varpi_{k+1}.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\omega\subset[n]
and collision parameters
(s_{k})_{k\leq n-|\omega|}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n-|\omega|}
. We define
\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}\subset\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}
as the set of initial parameters
Z_{n}
such that the collision graph of the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}(\cdot,Z_{n},\omega,(s_{k})_{k})
verifies the semi-tree condition with respect to
\omega
.

Figure 8
.
Here the pseudotrajectory checks the semi-tree condition, with
\omega_{1}=\{1\}
,
\varpi_{1}=\{1,2\}
,
\varpi_{2}=\{1,2,3,4\}
and
\varpi_{3}=\{1,2,3,4,5,6\}
. In the picture, when the trajectory of a particle is a dotted line, it does not overlap nor interact with any other particle (a crossing with a dotted line is an artifact of the one-dimensional drawing).

We need a new definition of pseudotrajectory:

Definition 3.10

(Third definition of pseudotrajectory)
.

Fix
m\leq n
. For a family of parameters
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m})
with
\omega_{1}\subset\omega_{2}\subset[n]
,
|\omega_{1}|=m
and
(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n-m}
, we define
{\rm Z}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k})
as

•

for
\tau\leq\delta,

{\rm Z}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m}):={\mathsf{Z}}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-|\omega_{2}|}),

•

for
\tau>\delta
,

{\rm Z}_{\omega_{2}}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m}):={\mathsf{Z}}(\tau-\delta,{\rm Z}_{\omega_{2}}(\delta),\omega_{1},(s_{k})_{n-|\omega_{2}|<k\leq n-m}),

and for all
i\in[n]\setminus\omega_{2}

{\rm z}_{i}(\tau):=({\rm x}_{i}(\delta)+(\tau-\delta){\rm v}_{i}(\delta),{\rm v}_{i}(\delta)).

Remark 3.6
.

Note that the particles in
[n_{2}]\setminus\omega_{2}
are virtual since time
\delta
: they do not interact with any other particle.

Definition 3.11
.

We define the collision graph
\mathscr{G}
of the pseudotrajectory
{\rm Z}(\cdot,Z_{n},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k})
. We define the graph
G^{\prime}
as the subgraph of
\mathscr{G}
with edges

\big\{(i,j)_{\tau,\sigma}\in E(\mathscr{G}),\penalty\ \tau\in[\delta,t],\penalty\ (i,j)\in\omega_{2}^{2}\big\}.

Fix
\omega_{1}\subset\omega_{2}\subset[n]
and collision parameters
(s_{k})_{k\leq n-|\omega_{1}|}
. We define the set

\mathscr{R}^{t}_{\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k}}\subset\left\{Z_{n}\in\mathscr{R}^{\delta}_{\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-|\omega_{2}|}}\Big|{\rm Z}(\cdot,Z_{n},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-|\omega_{1}|})\in\mathscr{R}^{\delta}_{\omega_{1},(s_{k})_{0\leq k-|\omega_{2}|\leq n-|\omega_{1}|}}\right\}

such that
G^{\prime}
has no cycle and verifies the semi-tree condition with respect to
\omega_{1}
.

Fix
m\leq n
,
\omega
such that
[m]\subset\omega\subset[n]
and
(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m}
some collision parameters.
We split
\mathscr{R}^{t}_{[m],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}
into two pieces:
\mathscr{R}^{>,t}_{[m],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}
where the collision graph has at least one cycle and
\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{[m],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}
where the collision graph has no cycle.

For
Z_{n}\in\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{[m],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}
, there are exactly
n-m
collisions in the collision graph, and
\omega
is not needed to reconstruct the pseudotrajectory. In addition, its collision graph checks the semi-tree conditions. Fixing the parameters
(s_{k})_{k}
, the sets
(\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{[m],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}})_{\omega}
are disjoint, as
\omega
is the union of the connected components of the vertices
1,\cdots,m
in the graph
G^{\prime}
. We deduce that

\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{[m],(s_{k})_{k}}=\bigsqcup_{[m]\subset\omega\subset[n]}\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{[m],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}.

We introduce now the functionals

(3.10)

\displaystyle\Psi^{0,t}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}]:=\frac{1}{(n_{1}-n_{0})!}\sum_{(s_{k})_{k\leq n_{1}-n_{0}}}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{1}-n_{0}}\!\!s_{k}\,h_{n_{0}}\left({\mathsf{Z}}_{[n_{0}]}(t,\cdot,[n_{0}],(s_{k})_{k})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{[n_{0}],(s_{k})_{k}}},

(3.11)

\displaystyle\Psi^{>,t}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}]:=\frac{1}{(n_{1}-n_{0})!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[n_{0}]\subset\omega\subset[n_{1}]\\
(s_{k})_{k\leq n_{1}-n_{0}}\end{subarray}}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{1}-n_{0}}\!\!s_{k}\,h_{n_{0}}\left({\rm Z}_{[n_{0}]}(t,\cdot,[n_{0}],\omega,(s_{k})_{k})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t}_{[n_{0}],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}}.

Fix
n_{0}\leq n_{1}\leq n_{2}
and a test function
h_{n_{0}}
. We obtain directly

\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\circ\Psi^{0,t}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}](Z_{n_{2}})\\
=\frac{1}{(n_{2}-n_{1})!(n_{1}-n_{0})!}\sum_{(s_{k})_{k\leq n_{2}-n_{0}}}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{2}-n_{0}}\!\!s_{k}\,h_{n_{0}}\left({\rm Z}_{[n_{0}]}(t+\delta,Z_{n_{2}},[n_{0}],[n_{1}],(s_{k})_{k})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{t+\delta}_{[n_{0}],[n_{1}],(s_{k})_{k}}}.

Then, summing on
n_{1}
and
\omega
,

\sum_{n_{1}=n_{0}}^{n_{2}}\frac{(n_{2}-n_{1})!(n_{1}-n_{0})!}{(n_{2}-n_{0})!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[n_{0}]\subset\omega\subset[n_{2}]\\
|\omega|=n_{1}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\circ\Psi^{0,t}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}](Z_{[n_{0}]},Z_{\omega\setminus[n_{0}]},Z_{[n_{2}]\setminus\omega})\\
\begin{split}&=\frac{1}{(n_{2}-n_{0})!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[n_{0}]\subset\omega\subset[n_{2}]\\
(s_{k})_{k\leq n_{2}-n_{0}}\end{subarray}}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{2}-n_{0}}\!\!s_{k}\,h_{n_{0}}\left({\rm Z}_{[n_{0}]}(t+\delta,Z_{n_{2}},[n_{0}],\omega,(s_{k})_{k})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{t+\delta}_{[n_{0}],\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}}\\
&=\Psi^{0,t+\delta}_{n_{0},n_{2}}[h_{n_{0}}](Z_{n_{2}})+\Psi^{>,t+\delta}_{n_{0},n_{2}}[h_{n_{0}}](Z_{n_{2}}).\end{split}

Using the symmetry of the summation set,

(3.12)

\sum_{n_{1}=n_{0}}^{n_{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\circ\Psi^{0,t}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}})\\
\begin{split}&=\sum_{n_{1}=n_{0}}^{n_{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}\binom{n_{2}-n_{0}}{n_{1}-n_{0}}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[n_{0}]\subset\omega\subset[n_{2}]\\
|\omega|=n_{1}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\circ\Psi^{0,t}_{n_{0},n_{1}}[h_{n_{0}}]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{[n_{0}]}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{\omega\setminus[n_{0}]}},{\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{[n_{2}]\setminus\omega}})\\
&=\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}\Psi^{0,t+\delta}_{n_{0},n_{2}}[h_{n_{0}}]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}})+\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}\Psi^{>,t+\delta}_{n_{0},n_{2}}[h_{n_{0}}]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{2}}}).\end{split}

The functional
\Psi^{0,t}_{m,n}
are introduced to implement the sampling: for
t>2\delta
and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}}\in\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}
(note that between the first and second lines, we use that by definition
\Phi_{1,n}^{0,\delta}=\Psi_{1,n}^{0,\delta}
)

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathscr{N}}h({\mathbf{z}}_{i}(t))&=\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Phi_{1,n}^{0,\delta}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t-\delta))+\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Phi_{1,n}^{>,\delta}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t-\delta))\\
&=\sum_{n^{\prime}\geq n\geq 0}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}\left(\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n,n^{\prime}}\Psi_{1,n}^{0,\delta}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}(t-2\delta))+\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n,n^{\prime}}\Psi^{0,\delta}_{1,n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}(t-k\delta))\right)\end{split}\\
+\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Phi_{1,n}^{>,\delta}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t-\delta)).

This can be rewritten as

\sum_{i=1}^{\mathscr{N}}h({\mathbf{z}}_{i}(t))=\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi_{1,n}^{0,t}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(0))+\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi^{>,k\delta}_{1,n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t-k\delta))\\
+\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{1\leq n\leq n^{\prime}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n,n^{\prime}}\circ\Psi^{0,(k-1)\delta}_{1,n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}(t-k\delta)).

The preceding computation can be iterated: for some time
t
,
\theta<t
and
\delta
such that
\theta/\delta=K\in\mathbb{N}
, and any initial data
{\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}}\in\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}

(3.13)

\sum_{i=1}^{\mathscr{N}}h({\mathbf{z}}_{i}(t))=\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi_{1,n}^{0,\theta}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t-\theta))+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi^{>,k\delta}_{1,n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t-k\delta))\\
+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{1\leq n\leq n^{\prime}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n,n^{\prime}}\circ\Psi^{0,(k-1)\delta}_{1,n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n^{\prime}}}(t-k\delta)).

3.5.
The decomposition of the covariance

The final ingredient is a second sampling on a longer time scale
\theta\simeq 1/\beta\log|\log\varepsilon|
, which controls the growth of the number of collisions.

Definition 3.12

(Number of particles at time
\tau
)
.

Fix
t
and
\delta
such that
t/\delta=K\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, parameters
(\{1\},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k\leq n-m})
and admissible initial data
Z_{n_{2}}\in\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{1\},\omega_{2},(s_{k})_{k}}
. We denote
\mathscr{G}
the collision graph. For
\tau=k\delta
, the
number of particles at time
\tau
,
\mathfrak{n}(\tau)
, is defined as the size of the connected component of
\{1\}
in the graph with edges

\{(i,j)|\exists(\sigma,\tau)\in\{\pm 1\}\times[k\delta,t]\penalty\ \text{such that}\penalty\ (i,j)_{\tau,\sigma}\in\mathscr{G}\}.

We want to control the number of particles
(\mathfrak{n}(t-k\theta))_{k}
such that it grows slower than the geometric sequence
2^{k}
.

Fix
1\leq n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{l}
. We denote
\underline{n}_{l}:=(n_{i})_{i\leq l}
. For
t\in((l-1)\theta,l\theta]

(3.14)

\Psi_{\underline{n}_{l}}^{0,t}[h]:=\frac{1}{(n_{l}-1)!}\sum_{(s_{k})_{k\leq n_{l}-1}}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{l}-1}s_{k}h({\rm z}_{1}(t,\cdot,\{1\},(s_{k})_{k}))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{1\},(s_{k})_{k}}}\prod_{i=1}^{\lfloor t/\theta\rfloor}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(t-i\theta)=n_{i}},\\

and for
t\in[(l-2)\theta,(l-1)\theta]

(3.15)

\Psi_{\underline{n}_{l}}^{>,t}[h]:=\frac{1}{(n_{l}-1)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\in\omega\subset[n_{l}]\\
(s_{k})_{k\leq n_{l}-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\prod_{k=1}^{n_{l}-1}s_{k}\,h({\rm z}_{1}(t,\cdot,\{1\},\omega,(s_{k})_{k}))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t}_{\{1\},\omega,(s_{k})_{k}}}\prod_{i=1}^{l-2}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(t-i\theta)=n_{i}}.

We can iterate the preceding decomposition of
\sum_{i=1}^{\mathscr{N}}h({\mathbf{z}}_{i}(t))
. The decomposition is performed until reaching the time
0
: denoting
K:=t/\theta\in\mathbb{N}
,
K^{\prime}:=\theta/\delta\in\mathbb{N}
,
\theta\simeq 1/\beta\log|\log\varepsilon|
(for
\beta
small enough), and
\delta\simeq\varepsilon^{1/12}
, for almost any initial data
{\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}}(0)\in\mathscr{D}
,

(3.16)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\mathscr{N}}h({\mathbf{z}}_{i}(t))=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq K}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{k}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}(t)\right)

(3.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq k\leq K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq k-1}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}\geq 2^{k}+n_{k-1}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}\Psi^{0,k\theta}_{\underline{n}_{k}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}(t-k\theta)\right)

(3.18)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq K-1\\
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq k}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k+2}\geq n_{k+1}\geq n_{k}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}\Psi^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}(t_{s})\right)

(3.19)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq K-1\\
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq k}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{k+1}\geq n_{k}\\
n_{k+2}\geq n_{k+1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}\Psi^{0,t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}(t_{s})\right)

where
t_{s}:=t-k\theta-k^{\prime}\delta
.

Finally, the covariance is split into five parts

(3.20)

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\zeta^{t}_{\varepsilon}(h)\zeta^{0}(g)\right]=G^{\text{main}}_{\varepsilon}(t)+G^{\text{clus}}_{\varepsilon}(t)+G^{\text{exp}}_{\varepsilon}(t)+G^{\text{rec},1}_{\varepsilon}(t)+G^{\text{rec},2}_{\varepsilon}(t),

where we have separated

•

the main part,

(3.21)

G^{\text{main}}_{\varepsilon}(t):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq K}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\Big({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}(0)\Big)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\right],

•

the first error due to the symmetric conditioning and the suppression of the overlaps,

(3.22)

G^{\text{clus}}_{\varepsilon}(t):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\zeta^{t}_{\varepsilon}(h)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\Big]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq K}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}(0)\right)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\right],

•

the part controlling the growth of the number of particles,

(3.23)

G^{\text{exp}}_{\varepsilon}(t):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\eqref{eq:partie avec trop de coll}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\right],

•

the part corresponding to non-local recollision,

(3.24)

G^{\text{rec},1}_{\varepsilon}(t):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\eqref{eq:partie des reco pas patho}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\right],

•

and the part corresponding to local recollision,

(3.25)

G^{\text{rec},2}_{\varepsilon}(t):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\eqref{eq:partie des reco patho}\times\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\right].

The parts
G^{\text{clus}}_{\varepsilon}(t)
and
G^{\text{exp}}_{\varepsilon}(t)
are estimated by (
5.1
):

\left|G^{\text{exp}}_{\varepsilon}(t)+G^{\text{rec}}_{\varepsilon}(t)\right|\leq C\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}(C\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K}}+\penalty\ \tfrac{\theta t^{2}}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}\right),

the part
G^{\text{rec},1}_{\varepsilon}(t)
is estimated by (
7.1
): for some
\mathfrak{a}>0
depending only on the dimension,

\left|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},1}(t)\right|\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}(Ct)^{2^{K}+d+9}\varepsilon^{{\mathfrak{a}}/2},

the part
G^{\text{rec},2}_{\varepsilon}(t)
is bounded at (
8.1
):

\Big|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},2}(t)\Big|\leq C\|h\|_{0}\|g\|_{0}(C\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}},

and the convergence of
G^{\text{main}}_{\varepsilon}(t)
is given by (
6.19
):

G^{\rm main}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{D}}h(z){\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t,z)M(z)dz+O\left(\left(C\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}K2^{K^{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\right)\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\right)

where
{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t,z)
is the solution of the linearized Boltzmann equation (
2.16
). Combining these four estimations and that
K\leq T/\theta
, we obtain the expected bound (
2.15
)

(3.26)

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{t}(g)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(h)\right]=\int_{\mathbb{D}}h(z){\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t,z)M(z)dz+O\left(\left(C\tfrac{\theta T^{2}}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}+\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}(\tfrac{CT}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{\frac{T}{\theta}+1}}\right)\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\right)

Remark 3.7
.

In this section, we have defined three different pseudotrajectories:

•

in Definition
3.3
we have defined the general definition of pseudotrajectory, which is used in the estimation of pathological recollision
G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},1}(t)
,

•

the pseudotrajectories of Definition
3.8
have no recollision and are used to treat
G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{main}}(t)
,
G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{clust}}(t)
and
G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{exp}}(t)
,

•

Definition
3.10
describes pseudotrajectories with non-pathological recollision. They are used to bound
G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},2}(t)
.

Remark 3.8

(Comparison with the strategy of
[
LB25
,
LB23
]
)
.

As explained in Remark
3.5
, we used in
[
LB25
]
a
tree pseudotrajectory
representation, while we use in the present article the graph pseudotrajectories. There are three advantages to this construction:

•

The tree pseudotrajectories have already been used in the description of gas interacting via compactly supported potential
[
Gra49
,
Kin75
,
GSRT13
,
PSS14
,
Ayi17
,
Cat18
]
. One of the encountered difficulties is the treatment of multiple interactions, which have to be treated separately from binary interactions even before imposing some conditioning. Our presentation avoids this difficulty.

•

In
[
LB25
]
, we needed two conditionings (see Section 2.3 of
[
LB25
]
). The first one is symmetric, and looks like
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}
(we denote it
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon}
) : we impose the same condition on all the particles. We also needed a second conditioning, which is asymmetric: we construct an indicator function
\mathscr{X}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}:\mathscr{D}\to\{0,1\}
to forbid a recollision on a small slice
[k\delta,(k+1)\delta]
that involves one of the particles of
{\underline{i}}_{n}
. Then we made the decomposition

\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})=\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})\mathscr{X}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}})+\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Psi_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})(1-\mathscr{X}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}})).

The use of the graph pseudotrajectories allows us to avoid this second conditioning.

•

In
[
LB25
]
, we used the following conditioning:
let
Z_{n}\in\mathbb{D}^{n}
be a particle configuration. We consider the graph
G
of vertices
\{1,\cdots,n\}
and of edges

\{(i,j)\in[1,m]^{2},\penalty\ d(x_{i},x_{j})<2\delta\mathbb{D}\}.

The coordinates
Z_{n}
form a
distance cluster
if the graph
G
is connected.

We construct the conditioning
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon}
as we have constructed
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}
, by replacing the notion of
possible cluster
by the distance clusters (we suppose that there is not a distance cluster larger than some integer
\gamma
).

The conditioning
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon}
is more constraining than
{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon}
: we obtained in Proposition 2.3 of
[
LB25
]

\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}\left({\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon}}^{c}\right)\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{t}{\delta}\left(\mu_{\varepsilon}\delta^{-1}\left(\mu_{\varepsilon}\delta^{d}\mathbb{V}^{d}\right)^{\gamma}+\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\gamma}e^{-\mathbb{V}^{2}/4}\right),

which is much larger than our estimation on
\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}^{c}_{\varepsilon}\right)
.

The conditioning
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon}
is only useful if we take
\delta
small enough (of order
\varepsilon^{1-\frac{1}{2d}}
for example). The problem is that the gain of smallness due to recollisions has to be smaller than
\delta
. If we restrict ourselves to the hard sphere setting, there is no problem, as one can obtain a gain of order
\varepsilon|\log\varepsilon|
(see the Appendix of
[
BGSRS23b
]
). In Assumption 2.3.1 of
[
LB23
]
, we proposed a family of interaction potentials for which the same estimation holds.

The cluster decomposition of this paper allows us to take a much larger
\delta=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{12}}
and then use the strategy of
[
PSS14
]
to bound the recollisions. This improvement is possible because we used the graph pseudo-trajectory.

4.
Quasi-orthogonality estimates

The different error terms obtained in the previous section are of the form

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Phi_{n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t_{s}))\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\right]

where the
\Phi_{n}:L^{\infty}(\mathbb{D})\to L^{\infty}(\mathbb{D}^{n})
are continuous functionals. In order to bound the errors, we need an
L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon})
bound of

\hat{\Phi}_{n}=\frac{1}{\mu^{n}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}\Phi_{n}[h]({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{n}].

The following section is dedicated to the derivation of such an estimate, using detailed estimations on the functionals
\Phi_{n}[h]
. We will use, in particular, that we can bound the
\Phi_{n}[h](Z_{n})
by looking only at the relative positions of particles inside
Z_{n}
.

Definition 4.1
.

We denote for
y\in{\mathbb{T}}
the translation operator

(4.1)

\tr_{y}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{~}c@{~}l}\mathbb{D}^{n}\penalty\ &\rightarrow\hfil\penalty\ &\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbb{D}^{n}\\
(X_{n},V_{n})\penalty\ &\mapsto\hfil\penalty\ &(x_{1}+y,\cdots,x_{n}+y,V_{n})\end{array}\right..

Fix
n,m
two integers,
g_{n}:\mathbb{D}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
,
h_{m}:\mathbb{D}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}
two functions, and
l\in[0,\min(n,m)]
. We define the multiplication on
l
variable
\circledast_{l}
as

(4.2)

g_{n}\circledast_{l}h_{m}(Z_{n+m-l})\\
:=\frac{1}{(n+m-l)!n!m!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}([n+m-l])\\
\sigma^{\prime}_{|[1,n]^{c}}={\rm Id}\\
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{|[n-l,2n-l]^{c}}={\rm Id}\end{subarray}}g_{n}(Z_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}([1,n])})h_{m}(Z_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime\prime}([n+1-l,n+m-l])}).

where
\mathfrak{S}(\omega)
is the group of permutation of
\omega
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Fix
m<n
two positive integers, and
g_{n}:\mathbb{D}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
,
h_{m}\mathbb{D}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}
two functions such that there exists a finite sequence
(c_{0},c_{0}^{\prime},c_{1},\cdots,c_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n+2}_{+}
bounding
g_{n},\,h_{m}
in the following way:

(4.3)

\int\limits_{x_{1}=0}\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|g_{n}\big(\tr_{y}Z_{n}\big)\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n}\leq c_{0},

(4.4)

\int\limits_{x_{1}=0}\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|h_{m}\big(\tr_{y}Z_{m}\big)\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{m}(Z_{m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{md}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,m}\,\mathrm{d}V_{m}\leq c^{\prime}_{0}

and for all
l\in[1,m]

(4.5)

\begin{split}\int\limits_{x_{1}=0}\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|g_{n}\circledast_{l}h_{m}\big(\tr_{y}Z_{n+m-l}\big)\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n+m-l}(Z_{n+m-l})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(n+m-l)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n+m-l}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n+m-l}\leq\frac{\mu^{l-1}}{n^{l}}c_{l}.\end{split}

There exists a constant
C>0
depending only on the dimension such that

(4.6)

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[g_{n}\big|=\int g_{n}(Z_{n})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H_{n}}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{nd/2}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}+O(C^{n}c_{0}\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}})

and

(4.7)

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu\hat{g}_{n}\hat{h}_{m}\Big]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{n}{l}\binom{m}{l}\frac{l!}{\mu^{l-1}}\int g_{n}\circledast_{l}h_{m}(Z_{n+m-l})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n+m-l}(Z_{n+m-l})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(n+m-l)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n+m-l}\\
+\,O\big(\big(c_{0}c^{\prime}_{0}+\max_{1\leq l\leq m}c_{l}\big)\,C^{n+m}\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}}\,\big).

In particular

(4.8)

\big|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[g_{n}\big]\big|=O(C^{n}c_{0}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \left|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[\mu\hat{g}_{n}\hat{h}_{m}\big]\right|\leq C^{n+m}\left(\max_{1\leq l\leq m}c_{l}+c_{0}c_{0}^{\prime}\,\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}}\right).

Remark 4.1
.

This theorem is the counterpart of Proposition 3.1 of
[
BGSRS23b
]
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

\bullet\penalty\ \penalty\
We begin with the proof of (
4.6
).

Using invariance of
\mathscr{H}_{p}
under permutation,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[g_{n}]&=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}\mu^{n}}\sum_{p\geq n}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\cdots i_{n})\\
\forall k,i_{k}\leq p\end{subarray}}g_{n}(Z_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})e^{-\mathscr{H}_{p}(Z_{p})}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}Z_{p}}{(2\pi)^{dp/2}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}\mu^{n}}\sum_{p\geq n}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\frac{p!}{(n-p!)}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})e^{-\mathscr{H}_{p}(Z_{p})}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}Z_{p}}{(2\pi)^{dp/2}}\\
&=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n+p}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{p})}M^{\otimes n}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}_{p}.\end{split}

We recall the notation

\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n}):=\alpha\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{x_{i}-x_{j}}{\varepsilon}\right)

and we denote in the following
\Omega:=\{X_{n},\underline{x}_{1},\cdots,\underline{x}_{p}\}
and for
X,Y\in\Omega
,

(4.9)

\varphi(\underline{x}_{i},\underline{x}_{j}):=\exp\left(-\alpha\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{\underline{x}_{i}-\underline{x}_{j}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)-1,\penalty\ \varphi(X_{N},\underline{x}_{j}):=\exp\left(-\alpha\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{{x}_{i}-\underline{x}_{j}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)-1.

Defining
d((x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}),(y_{1},\cdots,y_{m}))
as the minimum of the
|x_{i}-y_{j}|
, we can bound
\varphi
by

-\mathbbm{1}_{d(X,Y)<\varepsilon}\leq\varphi(X,Y)\leq 0.

We decompose
\exp\left({-\mathscr{V}_{n+p}(X_{n+1},\underline{X}_{p})}\right)

\begin{split}e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n+p}(X_{n+1},\underline{X}_{p})}&=e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}^{\varepsilon}(X_{n})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(X,Y)\in\Omega^{2}\\
X\neq Y\end{subarray}}\left(1+\varphi(X,Y)\right)=e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})}\sum_{G\in\mathscr{G}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(G)}\varphi(X,Y)\end{split}

where
\mathscr{G}(\Omega)
is the set of non-oriented graphs on
\Omega
and
E(G)
the set of edges of
G
. Denoting by
\mathscr{C}(\omega)
the set of connected graphs on
\omega
,

(4.10)

\begin{split}&\exp\left(-\mathscr{V}_{n+p}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{p})\right)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\subset[1,p]\end{subarray}}\Bigg(e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}G\in\mathscr{C}(\omega\cup\{X_{n}\})\end{subarray}}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(G)}\varphi(X,Y)\sum_{G\in\mathscr{G}([p]\setminus\omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(G)}\varphi(X,Y)\Bigg)\\
&=e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\subset[1,p]\end{subarray}}\Bigg(e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p-|\omega|}(\underline{X}_{[p]\setminus\omega})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}G\in\mathscr{C}(\omega\cup\{X_{n}\})\end{subarray}}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(G)}\varphi(X,Y)\Bigg)\\
&=:e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\subset[1,p]\end{subarray}}e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p-|\omega|}(\underline{X}_{[p]\setminus\omega})}\psi_{|\omega|}^{n}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{\omega})\;.\end{split}

Thus, using exchangeability,
\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[g_{n}]
is equal to

(4.11)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}=p}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\frac{p!}{p_{1}!p_{2}!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})\psi^{n}_{p_{1}}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{p_{1}})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p_{2}}(\underline{X}^{\prime}_{p_{2}})}\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\!\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\!\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p_{1}}}\!\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p_{2}}^{\prime}}\\
&=\left(\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p}(\underline{X}_{p})}\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p}}\right)\left(\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})\psi^{n}_{p}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{p})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})\psi^{n}_{p}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{p})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p}}.\end{split}

We recall Penrose’s tree inequality (see
[
Pen63
,
BGSRS23a
,
Jan
]
), for function
\varphi
defined in (
4.9
),

(4.12)

\left|\sum_{C\in\mathscr{C}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(C)}\varphi(X,Y)\right|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(T)}|\varphi(X,Y)|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(T)}\mathbbm{1}_{d(X,Y)<\varepsilon}

with
\mathscr{T}(\Omega)
the set of trees (minimally connected graphs) on
\Omega
. Fix
\tr_{-x_{1}}{X_{n}}
(the relative position between particles
1,\cdots,n
). Integrating a constraint
\varphi(\underline{x}_{i},\underline{x}_{j})
provides a factor
{\mathbf{c}}_{d}\varepsilon^{d}
,
\varphi(X_{n},\underline{x}_{j})
a factor
n{\mathbf{c}}_{d}\varepsilon^{d}
(where
{\mathbf{c}}_{d}
is the volume of a sphere of diameter
1
). As there are (see for example the Section 2 of
[
BGSRS23a
]
or
[
Jan
]
)

\frac{(p-1)!}{(d_{0}-1)!(d_{1}-1)!\cdots(d_{p}-1)!}

trees with specified vertex degrees
d_{0},\cdots,d_{p}
associated to vertices
X_{n},\,\underline{x}_{1},\cdots,\,\underline{x}_{p}
, we get

(4.13)

\begin{split}\bigg|\int\psi^{n}_{p}(&X_{n}\underline{X}_{p})\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p}}\bigg|\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1},\cdots,d_{p}\geq 1\\
d_{0}+\cdots+d_{p}=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{(p-1)!}{(d_{0}-1)!(d_{1}-1)!\cdots(d_{p}-1)!}n^{d_{0}}({\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon^{d})^{p}\\
&\leq(p-1)!({\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon^{d})^{p}\left(\sum_{d_{0}\geq 1}\frac{n^{d_{0}}}{(d_{0}-1)!}\right)\left(\sum_{d_{1}\geq 1}\frac{1}{(d_{1}-1)!}\right)\cdots\left(\sum_{d_{p}\geq 1}\frac{1}{(d_{p}-1)!}\right)\\
&\leq(p-1)!\,ne^{n}\big(e{\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon^{d}\big)^{p}.\end{split}

We can integrate on the rest of the parameters using (
4.3
). Hence, as
\psi_{0}^{n}(Z_{n})=\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{nd/2}}

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[g_{n}]|-\int g_{n}(Z_{n})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H_{n}}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{nd/2}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p\geq 1}\frac{(p-1)!\,ne^{n}\big(e{\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\mu\varepsilon^{d}\big)^{p}}{p!}\int|g_{n}(Z_{n})|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{0}C^{n}\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}}\sum_{p\geq 0}(C\varepsilon/\mathfrak{d})^{p}.

For some constant
C
depending only on the dimension. The series converges for
\varepsilon
small enough as
\mathfrak{d}\gg\varepsilon
. This concludes the proof of (
4.6
).

\bullet\penalty\ \penalty\
We treat now (
4.7
). Recall first that

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu\hat{g}_{n}\hat{h}_{m}\Big]=\frac{1}{\mu^{n+m-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}g_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})\sum_{\underline{j}_{m}}h_{m}({\mathbf{Z}}_{\underline{j}_{m}})\right]-\mu\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[g_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[h_{m}\right].

Let us count the number of ways such that
{\underline{i}}_{n}
and
\underline{j}_{m}
can intersect on a set of length
l
. We have to choose two sets
A\subset[n]
and
A^{\prime}\subset[m]
of length
l
, and a bijection
\sigma:A\to A^{\prime}
such that for all indices
k\in A
,
i_{k}=j_{\sigma{k}}
and that
{\underline{i}}_{A^{c}}
does not intersect
\underline{j}_{(A^{c})^{\prime}}
. Thus, using the symmetry,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu\,\hat{g}_{n}\hat{h}_{m}\Big]=&\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{n}{l}\binom{m}{l}\frac{l!}{\mu^{l-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[g_{n}\circledast_{l}h_{m}\big]\\
&+\mu\left(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\mu^{n+m}}\sum_{\underline{i}_{n+m}}g_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})h_{m}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n+1,n+m}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[g_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[h_{m}\right]\right).\end{split}

To estimate the error term in (
4.7
), we need to compute

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[&\frac{1}{\mu^{n+m}}\sum_{\underline{i}_{n+m}}g_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})h_{m}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n+1,n+m}})\Bigg]\\
&=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})h_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})\exp\left(-\mathscr{V}_{n+m+p}(X_{n},X^{\prime}_{m},\underline{X}_{p})\right)M^{\otimes n}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}M^{\otimes m}\,\mathrm{d}{Z^{\prime}_{m}}\,\mathrm{d}{\underline{X}_{p}}.\end{split}

We denote in the following

\Omega:=\{X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{x}_{1},\cdots,\underline{x}_{p}\}
,
and we decompose

\begin{split}\exp\left(-\mathscr{V}_{n+m+p}(X_{n},X^{\prime}_{m},\underline{X}_{p})\right)&=e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})}e^{-\mathscr{V}_{m}(X^{\prime}_{m})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(X,Y)\in\Omega^{2}\\
X\neq Y\end{subarray}}\left(1+\varphi(X,Y)\right)\\
&=e^{-\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})}e^{-\mathscr{V}_{m}(X^{\prime}_{m})}\sum_{G\in\mathscr{G}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(G)}\varphi(X,Y)\end{split}

where

\varphi(X_{n},X^{\prime}_{m}):=\exp\left(-\alpha\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{x_{i}-x^{\prime}_{j}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right).

We make a partition depending on the connected components of
X_{n}
and
X^{\prime}_{m}
in
G
,

\exp\left(-\mathscr{V}_{n+m+p}(X_{n},X^{\prime}_{m},\underline{X}_{p})+\mathscr{V}_{n}(X_{n})+\mathscr{V}_{m}(X^{\prime}_{m}))\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\subset[1,p]\end{subarray}}\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{\omega})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p-|\omega|}(\underline{X}_{[p]\setminus\omega})}\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{1}\subset[1,p]\\
\omega_{1}\cap\omega_{2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\psi^{n}_{|\omega_{1}|}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{\omega_{1}})\psi^{m}_{|\omega_{2}|}(X^{\prime}_{m},\underline{X}_{\omega_{2}})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p-|\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2}|}(\underline{X}_{[p]\setminus(\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2})})}.

where the first line corresponds to
X_{n}
and
X^{\prime}_{m}
in the same connected components, and second corresponds to
X_{n}
and
X^{\prime}_{m}
in disjoint connected components. In the preceding equation, we denote

\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{\omega})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}G\in\mathscr{C}(\omega\cup\\
\{X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime}\})\end{subarray}}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(G)}\varphi(X,Y).

Permutating the indices and using (
4.11
), we obtain the following equality:

\begin{array}[]{r@{}l@{}r}\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}&\lx@intercol\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})h_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1},\omega_{1}\subset[1,p]\\
\omega_{1}\cap\omega_{2}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\psi^{n}_{|\omega_{1}|}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{\omega_{1}})\psi^{m}_{|\omega_{2}|}(X^{\prime}_{m},\underline{X}_{\omega_{2}})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{|(\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2})^{c}|}(\underline{X}_{(\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2})^{c}})}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[-7.0pt]
&&\times\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{md}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z^{\prime}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}_{p}\\[5.0pt]
&\lx@intercol=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}=p}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\frac{p!}{p_{1}!p_{2}!p_{3}!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})h_{n^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}})\psi^{n}_{p_{1}}(X_{n},\underline{X}_{p_{1}})\psi^{1}_{p_{2}}(x_{n+1},\underline{X}^{\prime}_{p_{2}})\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&&\times\Big(\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{nd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n}d\underline{X}_{p_{1}}\Big)\Big(\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{md}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}^{\prime}_{p_{2}}\Big)\Big(e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p_{3}}(\underline{X}^{\prime\prime}_{p_{3}})}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}^{\prime\prime}_{p_{3}}\Big)\\
&\lx@intercol=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[g_{n}]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[h_{n^{\prime}}],\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

and in the same way

\begin{array}[]{r@{}l@{}r}\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}&\lx@intercol\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})h_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\subset[1,p]\end{subarray}}\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{\omega})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{||\omega^{c}|}(\underline{X}_{\omega^{c}})}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[-5.0pt]
&&\times\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})-\mathscr{H}_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(n+m)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}Z^{\prime}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}_{p}\\[10.0pt]
&\lx@intercol=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}}\sum_{p\geq 0}\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}=p}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}\frac{p!}{p_{1}!p_{2}!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})h_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{p_{1}})e^{-\mathscr{V}_{p_{2}}(\underline{X}^{\prime}_{p_{2}})}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&&\times\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})-\mathscr{H}_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(n+m)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}Z^{\prime}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}_{p_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}^{\prime}_{p_{2}}\\[13.0pt]
&\lx@intercol=\sum_{p_{1}\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p_{1}!}\int g_{n}(Z_{n})h_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{p_{1}})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})-\mathscr{H}_{m}(Z^{\prime}_{m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(n+m)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}Z^{\prime}_{m}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}_{p_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\underline{X}^{\prime}_{p_{2}}.\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

We will use again the Penrose tree inequality,

\left|\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{p_{1}})\right|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(T)}|\varphi(X,Y)|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}(\Omega)}\prod_{(X,Y)\in E(T)}\mathbbm{1}_{d(X,Y)<\varepsilon}.

First, we fix
\tr_{-x_{1}}{X_{n}}
and
\tr_{-x^{\prime}_{1}}{X^{\prime}_{m}}
. Integrating a constraint
\varphi(\underline{x}_{i},\underline{x}_{j})
provides a factor
{\mathbf{c}}_{d}\varepsilon^{d}
,
\varphi(X_{n},\underline{x}_{j})
a factor
n{\mathbf{c}}_{d}\varepsilon^{d}
,
\varphi(X^{\prime}_{m},\underline{x}_{j})
a factor
m{\mathbf{c}}_{d}\varepsilon^{d}
, and
\varphi(X_{n},X^{\prime}_{m})
a factor
nm{\mathbf{c}}_{d}\varepsilon^{d}
. Denoting
d_{0},d^{\prime}_{0},d_{1}\cdots,

d_{p}
the degrees of
X_{n},\,X_{m}^{\prime},\,\underline{x}_{1},\cdots,\,\underline{x}_{m}
and
\hat{x}_{1}:=x_{1}-x^{\prime}_{1}
,

(4.14)

\begin{split}\bigg|\int\psi^{n,m}_{|\omega|}&(X_{n},X_{m}^{\prime},\underline{X}_{p_{1}})d\underline{X}_{p}d\hat{x}_{1}\bigg|\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{\prime}_{0},d_{0},\cdots,d_{p}\geq 1\\
d^{\prime}_{0}+d_{0}+\cdots+d_{p}=2p\end{subarray}}\frac{p!}{(d^{\prime}_{0}-1)(d_{0}-1)!\cdots(d_{p}-1)!}n^{d_{0}}m^{d^{\prime}_{0}}({\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon^{d})^{+1}\\
&\leq p!\big({\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon^{d}\big)^{p+1}nm\,e^{n+m+p}.\end{split}

We can integrate on the rest of the parameters using (
4.3
) and (
4.4
), and finally

\begin{split}&\mu\left(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\mu^{n+m}}\sum_{\underline{i}_{n+m}}g_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}})h_{m}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n+1,n+m}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[g_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[g\right]\right).\\
&\leq c_{0}c_{0}^{\prime}\mu\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{\mu^{p}}{p!}p!\big({\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon^{d}\big)^{p+1}nm\,e^{n+m+p}\\
&\leq\mu\varepsilon^{d}nm({\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}e)^{n+m}c_{0}c_{0}^{\prime}\sum_{p\geq 0}(e{\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon)^{p}\\
&\leq(\varepsilon/\mathfrak{d})C^{n+m+1}\sum_{p\geq 0}(e{\mathbf{c}}_{{\mathbf{d}}}\varepsilon/\mathfrak{d})^{p}\end{split}

which converges for
\varepsilon
small enough.

\bullet\penalty\ \penalty\
To conclude the proof, we apply the estimation (
4.6
) to (
4.7
):

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu\,\hat{g}_{n}\hat{h}_{m}\Big]=&\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{n}{l}\binom{m}{l}\frac{l!}{\mu^{l-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[g_{n}\circledast_{l}h_{m}\big]+O(C^{n+m}c_{0}c^{\prime}_{0}\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}})\\
=&\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{n}{l}\binom{m}{l}\frac{l!}{\mu^{l-1}}\int g_{n}\circledast_{l}h_{m}(Z_{n+m-l})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n+m-l}(Z_{n+m-l})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(n+m-l)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n+m-l}\\
&+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}}\left(C^{n+m}c_{0}c^{\prime}_{0}+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{n}{l}\binom{m}{l}\frac{l!}{\mu^{l-1}}\frac{\mu^{l-1}C^{l}}{n^{l}}c_{l}\right)\right).\end{split}

Then,

\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{n}{l}\binom{m}{l}\frac{l!}{\mu^{l-1}}\frac{\mu^{l-1}C^{l}}{n^{l}}c_{l}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{m}\binom{m}{l}\frac{n!}{(n-l)!n^{l}}C^{l}c_{l}\ \leq(1+C)^{m}\max_{1\leq l\leq m}c_{l}

which concludes the proof.

\bullet\penalty\ \penalty\
The inequalities (
4.8
) can be obtained in the same way.

∎

Note also the following bound in
L^{p}
norms of the fluctuation field.

Proposition 4.2
.

For any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C_{p}>0
such that

(4.15)

\left(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)^{p}\right]\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{p}\|g\|_{L^{p}(M(v)dz)}.

The proof can be found in Appendix A of
[
BGSRS23b
]
.

From these estimations, one can deduce the following corollary:

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
h_{n}
be a test function satisfying the conditions of Proposition
4.1
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(4.16)

\begin{split}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}&h_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t_{s}))\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]\Bigg|\leq C^{n}\mu^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)^{2}\big]^{1/2}\left(c_{0}+\left(\sup_{1\leq l\leq n}c_{l}\right)^{1/2}\right).\end{split}

Proof.

We can decompose the left hand side as

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}&\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}h_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t_{s}))\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]=\mu^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\mu^{\frac{1}{2}-n}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}h_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t_{s}))\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]\\
&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ =\mu^{n-1}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{h_{n}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}}(t_{s}))\,\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[h_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Big]\bigg)\\
&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ =\mu^{n-1}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{h_{n}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathscr{N}}(t_{s}))\,\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[h_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon}})\right)\Big]\bigg)\;.\end{split}

Using that
\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)]=0
. Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\begin{split}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[&\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}h_{n}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n}}(t_{s}))\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
&\leq\mu^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\mu\left[\widehat{h_{n}}\right]^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[h_{n}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\big[\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mu\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}\big[\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}^{c}\big]\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\;.\end{split}

We apply now Proposition
4.1
. The bound on
\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}\left[\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}^{c}\right]
given in Section
3.2
and the bound on the
L^{p}
norm of
\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)
(
4.15
) lead to the stated corollary.
∎

5.
Clustering estimations without recollision

The objective of this section is to bound
G^{\text{clust}}_{\varepsilon}(t)
and
G^{\text{exp}}_{\varepsilon}(t)
, defined by

\begin{split}G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{clust}}(t):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\zeta^{t}_{\varepsilon}(h)&\zeta^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\Big]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq K}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}(0)\right)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\right],\end{split}

G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{exp}}(t):=\sum_{1\leq k\leq K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq k-1}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}\geq 2^{k}+n_{k-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}\Psi^{0,k\theta}_{\underline{n}_{k}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}(t-k\theta)\right)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\right].

Proposition 5.1
.

For
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

(5.1)

\left|G^{\text{exp}}_{\varepsilon}(t)+G^{\text{rec}}_{\varepsilon}(t)\right|\leq C\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\left(\varepsilon^{1/3}(Ct)^{2^{t/\theta}}+\penalty\ \tfrac{\theta t^{2}}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}\right)

To obtain the stated result, we need first the following bounds on the pseudotrajectory developments without recollisions of type
\Psi_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h]
:

Proposition 5.2
.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\underline{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
with
n_{1}\leq n_{2}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k}
. Then, fixing
x_{0}=0
,

(5.2)

\int\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h](\tr_{y}Z_{n_{k}})\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{n_{k}})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{k}d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k}}\leq\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}-1}}C^{n_{k}}\theta^{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}(k\theta)^{n_{k-1}-1},

and, for
m\in[1,n_{k}]
,

(5.3)

\begin{split}\int\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h]\circledast_{m}{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h](\tr_{y}Z_{2n_{k}-m})\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k}-m}(Z_{2n_{k}-m})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(2n_{k}-m)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{2n_{K}-m}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,2n_{K}-m}\\
\leq\frac{\mu^{m-1}}{n_{k}^{m}}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}-1}}C^{n_{k}}\right)^{2}\theta^{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}(k\theta)^{n_{k-1}+n_{k}-1}.\end{split}

Using Corollary
4.3
and the previous estimations,

\begin{split}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}\Psi_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{\underline{i}_{n_{k}}}(t-k\theta)\right)\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]\Bigg|&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}C^{n_{k}}\left((\tfrac{\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}(\tfrac{k\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k-1}-1}\right.\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left.(\tfrac{\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}(\tfrac{k\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{\frac{n_{k}+n_{k-1}-1}{2}}\right)\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}C^{n_{k}}(\tfrac{\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}(\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}},\end{split}

and in the same way,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}&\Psi_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{\underline{i}_{n_{K}}}(0)\right)\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]=O\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}C^{n_{k}}(t/\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}}\right).\end{split}

Summing over all possible
(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})
, we obtain

\begin{split}\left|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{exp}}(t)\right|&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k-1}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}>2^{k}+n_{k-1}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}C^{n_{k}}(\theta/\mathfrak{d})^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}(t/\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}}\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k-1}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}>2^{k}+n_{k-1}}\left(C\tfrac{\theta t^{2}}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}\right)^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}\\
&\leq C\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\sum_{k=1}^{K}2^{k^{2}}\left(C\tfrac{\theta t^{2}}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}\right)^{2^{k-1}}\leq C\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\tfrac{\theta t^{2}}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}.\end{split}

as the series converges for
\theta
small enough. In the same way

\begin{split}\left|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{clust}}(t)\right|\leq&\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}(\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)^{4}]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}[\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(h)^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\quad+\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{K}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\!\!\mathbb{P}_{\varepsilon}(\Upsilon^{c}_{\varepsilon})^{\frac{1}{4}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}C^{n_{k}}t^{n_{k}}+\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}(\tfrac{C\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{K}}(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq&C\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}2^{K^{2}}(Ct)^{2^{K}}.\end{split}

This concludes the proof of (
5.1
).

Proof of (
5.2
).

We recall that for
t=k\theta
and that

{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,k\theta}[h]:=\frac{1}{(n_{k}-1)!}\sum_{(s_{l})_{l\leq n_{k}-1}}\prod_{l=1}^{n_{k}-1}s_{l}h({\mathsf{z}}_{1}(t,\cdot,\{1\},(s_{l})_{l}))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q\},(s_{l})_{l}}}\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(i\theta)=n_{n_{k-i}}}.

This gives directly the following bound on
\bar{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,t}[h]

(5.4)

\left|\bar{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,t}[h]\right|\leq\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(n_{k}-1)!}\sum_{(s_{l})_{l\leq n_{k}-1}}\sum_{(s_{l})_{l\leq n_{k}-1}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{1\},(s_{l})_{l}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)=n_{k-1}}.

As the right-hand side of (
5.4
) is invariant under translations, it is sufficient to fix
x_{1}=0
and integrate with respect to
(X_{2,n_{k}},V_{n_{k}})
.

We define the
clustering tree

T^{>}
as the sequence
(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n_{k}-1}
where the
i
-th collision involves particles
q_{i}
and
\bar{q}_{i}
(and
q_{i}<\bar{q}_{i}
).

Since in the present section, pseudotrajectories have no recollision, the collision graph is a tree (a simply connected graph). Hence, we can construct the clustering by forgetting the scattering times associated with each edge but keeping the order of the collisions.
It can be used to parametrize a partition of
\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q\},(s_{l})_{l}}
.

Let us fix a clustering tree. We perform the following change of variables

X_{2,n_{k}}\mapsto(\hat{x}_{1},\cdots,\hat{x}_{n_{k}-1}),\penalty\ \forall i\in[1,n_{k}-1],\penalty\ \hat{x}_{i}:=x_{q_{i}}-x_{\bar{q}_{i}}

Fix then
\tau_{i+1}
the time of the
(i+1)
-th collision, as well as the relative positions
\hat{x}_{1},\cdots,\hat{x}_{i-1}
. We denote
T_{i}=\theta
if
i\leq n_{k}-n_{k-1}
,
t
else (at least
n_{k}-n_{k-1}
clustering collisions happen before time
\theta
). The
i
-th collision set is defined by

B_{T^{>},i}:=\left\{\hat{x}_{i}\Big|\exists\tau\in(0,T_{i}\wedge\tau_{i+1}),\penalty\ |{\mathsf{x}}_{q_{i}}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau)|\leq\varepsilon\right\}.

Because particles
{\mathsf{x}}_{q_{i}}(\tau)
and
{\mathsf{x}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau)
are independent until their first meeting, we can perform the change of variable
\hat{x}_{i}\mapsto(\tau_{i},\eta_{i})
where
\tau_{i}
is the first meeting time and

\eta_{i}:=\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{x}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})}{\left|{\mathsf{x}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{x}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right|}.

It sends the Lebesgue measure
\!\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{i}
to the measure
\varepsilon^{d-1}(({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}))\cdot\eta_{i})_{+}\,\mathrm{d}\eta_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}
and

\int\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T^{>},i}}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{i}\leq{C}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}\int_{0}^{T_{i}\wedge\tau_{i+1}}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right|\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}.

We want now to sum on every possible edge
(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})
. Hence, we need to control

\sum_{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right|\leq 2n_{k}\sum_{k}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{k}(\tau_{i})\right|\leq 2n_{k}\left(n_{k}\sum_{k}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{k}(\tau_{i})\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq n_{k}\left(n_{k}+|{\mathsf{V}}_{n_{k}}(\tau_{i})|^{2}\right)

Lemma 5.3
.

Consider a time
\tau\in[0,t]
, collision parameters
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{i})_{i})
and an initial position
Z_{n}\in\mathbb{D}^{n}
. Then

\tfrac{1}{2}|{\mathsf{V}}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{i})_{i})|^{2}\leq\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n}),

as there is no overlap between particles.

Proof.

We begin by defining the notion of
clusters on a time segment
:

Definition 5.1
.

Consider two times
0\leq\tau_{a}<\tau_{b}\leq t
. We denote
\mathscr{G}
the collision graph of the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}_{n_{k}}(\cdot,(\omega,(s_{i})_{i}),Z_{n_{k}})
on the time interval
[\tau_{a},\tau_{b}]
and
G
a graph with edges

\Big\{(q,\bar{q})\in[n]^{2},\penalty\ \exists\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau_{a},\tau_{b}],\penalty\ (q,\bar{q})_{1,\tau}\in\mathscr{G}\}.

We take only into account the collisions with interaction. We define
\underline{\kappa}:=(\kappa_{1},\cdots,\kappa_{k})
the
clusters on the segment
[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]
the connected components of
G
(defined in the following of Definition
3.8
).

Note that if
\tau_{a}
lies between the beginning of the collision implying
s_{j}
and the beginning of the collision implying
s_{j+1}
, then
\underline{\kappa}
only depends on the
(s_{i})_{i\leq j}
.

We distinguished the cases
\tau>\delta
and
\tau\leq\delta.

•

First, if
\tau\leq\delta
. We consider
(\kappa_{1},\cdots,\kappa_{k})
the cluster on the segment
[0,\delta]
constructed in the following Definition
3.8
. The pseudotrajectory is the Hamiltonian trajectory associated with the energy

\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}}(Z_{n}):=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Bigg(\sum_{q\in\kappa_{i}}\frac{|v_{q}|^{2}}{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q,\bar{q}\in\kappa_{i}\\
q\neq\bar{q}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha}{2}\mathscr{V}\left(\frac{x_{q}-x_{\bar{q}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\Bigg).

Hence

\tfrac{1}{2}|{\mathsf{V}}(\tau,Z_{n},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(s_{i})_{i})|^{2}\leq\mathscr{H}^{\underline{\kappa}}({\mathsf{Z}}_{n}(\tau)\leq\mathscr{H}^{\underline{\kappa}}(Z_{n})\leq\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n}).

•

If
\tau>\delta
, consider
\underline{\kappa}
and
\underline{\kappa}^{\prime}
the clusters on
[\delta,\tau]
and on
[0,\delta]
. After time
\delta
, the particles outside
\omega_{2}
stop interacting, and before time
\delta
, the couple of particles in
\omega_{2}
cannot overlap. Hence,
\underline{\kappa}^{\prime}
is a finer partition of
[n]
than
\underline{\kappa}
and
\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}}\leq\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}^{\prime}}
. Thus

(5.5)

\frac{1}{2}|{\mathsf{V}}_{n_{k}}(\tau)|^{2}\leq\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}}({\mathsf{Z}}_{n_{k}}(\tau))=\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}}({\mathsf{Z}}_{n_{k}}(\delta))\leq\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{Z}}_{n_{k}}(\delta))=\mathscr{H}_{\underline{\kappa}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{Z}}_{n_{k}}(0))\leq\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n}).

∎

As we suppose that there is no overlap at time
0
, we have
\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})=\tfrac{|V_{n}|^{2}}{2}
. Hence, using the Boltzmann-Grad scaling
\mu\varepsilon^{d-1}\mathfrak{d}=1
,

\begin{split}\sum_{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})_{i}}&\int\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{1}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T^{>},1}}\cdots\int\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{n_{k}-1}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T^{>},n_{k}-1}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)=n_{k-1}}e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{n_{k}})}\\
\leq&\left(\frac{Cn_{k}}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{n_{k}-1}\left(n_{k}+\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{n_{k}})\right)^{n_{k}-1}e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{n_{k}})}\int_{0}^{T_{n_{k}-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{n_{k}-1}\cdots\int_{0}^{T_{1}\wedge\tau_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}\\
\leq&\left(\frac{Cn_{k}}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{n_{k}-1}n_{k}^{n_{k}-1}e^{-\frac{\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{n_{k}})}{2}}\frac{t^{n_{k-1}-1}}{(n_{k-1}-1)!}\frac{\theta^{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}}{(n_{k}-n_{k-1})!}\\
\leq&\left(\frac{\tilde{C}}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{n_{k}-1}n_{k}^{n_{k}-1}e^{-\frac{|V_{n_{k}}|^{2}}{4}}t^{n_{k-1}-1}\theta^{n_{k}-n_{k-1}},\end{split}

We used these two classical inequalities

\displaystyle\frac{(a+b)^{a+b}}{a!b!}\leq e^{a+b}

\displaystyle\frac{(a+b)!}{a!b!}\leq(2e)^{a+b}

\displaystyle\forall A,B>0,\penalty\ ,x\in\mathbb{R}^{+},\penalty\ \penalty\ \left(A+x\right)^{B}e^{-\frac{x}{2}}=

\displaystyle B^{B}\left(\frac{A+x}{B}e^{-\frac{A+x}{2B}}\right)^{B}e^{\frac{A}{4}}\leq\left(\tfrac{4B}{e}\right)^{B}e^{\frac{A}{4}}.

Finally, we sum on
V_{n_{k}}
, on the
2^{n_{k}-1}
possible
(s_{i})_{i}
and on the
q\in[1,n_{k}]
, and we divide by the remaining
(n_{k}!)
. This gives the expected estimation.
∎

Proof of (
5.3
).

We begin as in the previous paragraph. Using that

\left|\left\{(\omega,\omega^{\prime},q,q^{\prime})\big|\penalty\ \omega\cup\omega^{\prime}=[2n_{k}-m],\penalty\ |\omega|=|\omega^{\prime}|=n_{k},\penalty\ q\in\omega,\penalty\ q^{\prime}\in\omega^{\prime}\right\}\right|=\frac{n_{k}^{2}(2n_{k}-m)!}{(n_{k}-m)!^{2}m!},

and that the right hand side of (
5.4
) is symmetric, one has

(5.6)

\Big|{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,t}[h]\circledast_{m}\bar{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k}}^{0,t}[h](Z_{2n_{k}-m})\Big|\\
\leq\frac{\|h\|_{0}^{2}}{(n_{k}!)^{2}}\frac{(n_{k}-m)!^{2}m!}{n_{k}^{2}(2n_{k}-m)!}\sum_{(\omega,\omega^{\prime},q,q^{\prime})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s_{l})_{l\leq n_{k}-1}\\
(s^{\prime}_{l})_{l\leq n_{k}-1}\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q\},(s_{l})_{l}}}(Z_{\omega})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)={n_{k-1}}}(Z_{\omega})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q^{\prime}\},(s^{\prime}_{l})_{l}}}(Z_{\omega^{\prime}}).

where
\mathfrak{n}(\theta)
is the number of particles at time
\theta
in the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}(t,\cdot,\{1\},(s_{l})_{l})
. The right-hand side is invariant under translation. Hence, without loss of generality, we can suppose that
1\notin\omega^{\prime}
and fix
x_{1}=0
.

We have to consider two pseudotrajectories

{\mathsf{Z}}(\tau):={\mathsf{Z}}(\tau,Z_{\omega},\{q\},(s_{l})_{l}){\rm\penalty\ and\penalty\ }{\rm Z}^{\prime}(\tau):={\mathsf{Z}}(\tau,Z_{\omega^{\prime}},\{q^{\prime}\},(s^{\prime}_{l})_{l}).

We want to estimate

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q^{\prime}\},(s^{\prime}_{l})_{l}}}(Z_{\omega^{\prime}})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k}-m}(Z_{2n_{k}-m})}\,\mathrm{d}{Z_{\omega^{\prime}\setminus\omega}}.

Fix
Z_{\omega}
and denote
T_{a}
the clustering tree of the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}(t)
, constructed as in the proof of (
5.2
). Next, we construct the clustering tree associated with the second pseudotrajectory: let
(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})_{i\leq\ell}
be the edges of the collision graph of
{\mathsf{Z}}^{\prime}(\tau)
, taking temporal order. Set
\bar{T}_{0}=\emptyset
. Suppose that
\bar{T}_{i}
is constructed. Then
\bar{T}_{i+1}:=\bar{T}_{i}\cup\{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})\}
if the graph
T_{a}\cup\bar{T}_{i}\cup\{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})\}
has no cycle. Else
\bar{T}_{i+1}:=\bar{T}_{i}
. At the
\ell
-step we have constructed an ordered graph
T_{b}:=\bar{T}_{\ell}
with
n_{k}-m
edges.

The
T_{b}
define a partition of
\{Z_{\omega^{\prime}\setminus\omega}\in\mathbb{D}^{n_{k}-m}|Z_{\omega^{\prime}}\in\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q^{\prime}\},(s^{\prime}_{l})_{l}}\}
where the coordinates
Z_{\omega}
are fixed.

The rest of the proof is almost identical to the proof of (
5.2
). Fix the clustering tree
T_{b}=(q_{i},

\bar{q_{i}})_{n_{k}\leq i\leq 2n_{k}-m}
, and perform the following change of variables

X_{\omega^{\prime}\setminus\omega}\mapsto(\hat{x}_{n_{k}},\cdots,\hat{x}_{2n_{k}-m-1}),\penalty\ \forall i\in[n_{k},2n_{k}-m-1],\penalty\ \hat{x}_{i}:=x_{q_{i}}-x_{\bar{q}_{i}}.

Fix
\tau_{i+1}
, the time of the
(i+1)
-th collision, and relative positions
\hat{x}_{n_{k}},\cdots,\hat{x}_{i-1}
. We define the
i
-th collision set as

B_{T^{>},i}:=\left\{\hat{x}_{i}\Big|\exists\tau\in(0,t\wedge\tau_{i+1}),\penalty\ |{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}_{q_{i}}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau)|\leq\varepsilon\right\}.

As in the preceding lemma, we can perform the change of variable
\hat{x}_{i}\mapsto(\tau_{i},\eta_{i})
where
\tau_{i}
is the first meeting time and

\eta_{i}:=\frac{{\mathsf{x^{\prime}}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{x^{\prime}}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})}{\varepsilon}.

We have

\begin{split}\sum_{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})}\int&\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T^{>},i}}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{i}\leq{C}{\varepsilon^{d-1}}\int_{0}^{\tau_{i+1}}\sum_{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})\in\omega^{\prime 2}}\left|{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right|\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}.\end{split}

Using the same method as in the proof of (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})\in\omega^{\prime 2}}\Big|{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\Big|\leq n_{k}+\left|{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{\omega^{\prime}}(\tau_{i})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2n_{k}+2\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{\omega^{\prime}}).

Using that

\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k}-m}(Z_{2n_{k}-m})\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{\omega})+\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{\omega^{\prime}})\right),

we can apply the same computation as above,

\begin{split}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q^{\prime}\},(s^{\prime}_{l})_{l}}}(Z_{\omega^{\prime}})e^{-\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k}-m}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{\omega^{\prime}\setminus\omega}&\leq\sum_{T_{b}}\int e^{-\frac{\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{\omega})}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k}-m}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T^{>},i}}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{i}\penalty\ e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k}}(Z_{\omega^{\prime}})}dV_{\omega^{\prime}}\\
&\leq C\left(\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{n_{k}-m}t^{n_{k}-m}(2n_{k}-m)^{n_{k}-m}.\end{split}

We can estimate

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{q\},(s_{l})_{l}}}(Z_{\omega})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k}-m}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{\omega\setminus\{1\}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\omega}

as in the proof of (
5.2
). We get the expected result by summing on all the possible parameters
(s_{i})_{i}
,
(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}
,
q
,
q^{\prime}
,
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
.
∎

6.
Treatment of the main part

The aim of this section is
the proof of

G^{\rm main}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{D}}h(z){\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t,z)M(z)dz+O\left(\left(C\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\right)\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\right),

where
{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t,z)
is the solution of the linearized Boltzmann equation (
2.16
) and
\mathfrak{a}\in(0,1)
is some fix constant depending only on the dimensio
n.

6.1.
Duality formula

We recall that

\begin{split}G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{main}}(t)\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ &=\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq K}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{K}}}(0)\right)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\right]\\[0.0pt]
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{K}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\mu^{n_{K}}\,\hat{\Psi}^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\,\hat{g}\right]\end{split}

where
\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]
is the development of
h(z_{i}(t))
along pseudotrajectories with
n_{k}
remaining particles at time
t-k\theta
, and neither recollision nor overlap nor multiple encounters
.

We denote

(6.1)

g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{K}}g(z_{i})

Then, using the equality (
4.7
) and
L^{1}
estimations on
\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]
of Section
5
, we have for
h
and
g
in
L^{\infty}

\begin{split}&G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{main}}(t)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{K}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\int\mu^{n_{K}-1}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left(Z_{n_{K}}\right)g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})\,\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{K}d}{2}}}+O\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{n}\end{subarray}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}}\|h\|_{0}\|g\|_{0}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{n}_{K}\end{subarray}}\int\mu^{n_{K}-1}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left(Z_{n_{K}}\right)g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})\,\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{K}d}{2}}}+O\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\mathfrak{d}}\left(K2^{K^{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\|h\|_{0}\|g\|_{0}\right)\right).\end{split}

We want to compute the asymptotics of each term in the sum. As we suppose that there is no overlap

\begin{split}&\int\mu_{\varepsilon}^{n_{K}-1}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left(Z_{n_{K}}\right)g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{K}d}{2}}}\\
&=\frac{\mu^{n_{K}-1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s_{k})_{k}\end{subarray}}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{l}-1}s_{k}\int_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{1\},(s_{k})_{k}}}\!\!\!\!h({\mathsf{z}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,Z_{n_{K}},\{1\},(s_{k})_{k}))g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})\prod_{i=1}^{K}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(t-i\theta)=n_{i}}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}.\end{split}

where
\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{1\},(s_{k})_{k}}
is the set of initial parameters such that the pseudotrajectory has no recollision and
\mathfrak{n}(\tau)
is the number of remaining particles at time
\tau
(see definition
3.12
). We had an exponent
\varepsilon
on
{\mathsf{z}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}
to mark the
\varepsilon
-dependence of the pseudotrajectory.

We want to construct the limiting process of the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)
.

We denote
{T}
the
clustering tree
as the sequence
(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},\bar{s}_{i})_{i\leq n_{K}-1}
such that the
i
-th collision happens between particles
q_{i}
and
\bar{q}_{i}
(with
q_{i}<\bar{q}_{i}
) and
\bar{s}_{i}
is equal to
1
(respectively
-1
) if the particles interact (respectively do not interact). Fixing the initial velocities
V_{n_{K}}
, we perform the change of variable

X_{n_{K}}\mapsto(x_{1},(\nu_{i},\tau_{i})_{i\leq n_{K}-1}),.

where
\tau_{i}
is the first time when particles
q_{i}
and
\bar{q}_{i}
meet, an

\nu_{i}=\frac{{\mathsf{x}}^{\varepsilon}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{x}}^{\varepsilon}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})}{\varepsilon}.

The Jacobian of this application is

\,\mathrm{d}X_{n_{K}}\to\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k}-1}\varepsilon^{d-1}\left(\left({\mathsf{v}}^{\varepsilon}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{\varepsilon}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right)\cdot\nu_{i}\right)_{+}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}=:\frac{\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\penalty\ \,\mathrm{d}x_{1}

where we have denoted

\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{n_{k}-1}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \tau_{[n_{k}-1]}=(\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{n_{k}-1}).

The kernel
\Lambda(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})
only depends on the successive velocities
({\mathsf{v}}^{\varepsilon}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}),{\mathsf{v}}^{\varepsilon}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}))
which can be deduced from the collision graph, forgetting the exact values of the
\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{k}-1]}
(since we have forbidden the pathological pseudotrajectories).

We defined the signature of the collision tree
\sigma(T):=\bar{s}_{1}\bar{s}_{2}\cdots\bar{s}_{n_{K}}
, the set of collision times

\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}:=\left\{(\tau_{i})_{i\leq n_{K}-1},\penalty\ \tau_{i}\leq\tau_{i+1},\penalty\ \forall k\leq K,\penalty\ j\in[n_{K}-n_{K-k},n_{K}-n_{K-k-1}],\penalty\ k\theta\leq\tau_{j}\leq(k+1)\theta\right\}

and for a given family
\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}
, we define
\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}(\tau_{[n_{K}-1]})
the set of coordinates
(x_{1},(\nu_{i})_{i\leq n_{K}-1},V_{n_{K}})
such that the pseudotrajectory has no recollision, and for all
j
,
\left({\mathsf{v}}^{\varepsilon}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{\varepsilon}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right)\cdot\nu_{i}
is positive.

The map

\begin{array}[]{c}\bigsqcup_{(s_{k})_{k}}\{s_{k}\}\times\left(\mathscr{R}^{0,t}_{\{1\},(s_{k})_{k}}\cap\{\text{no\penalty\ overlap}\}\cap\bigcap_{j\leq K-1}\{\mathfrak{n}(j\theta)=n_{K-j}\big\}\right)\to\bigsqcup_{T}\{T\}\times\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}\\
(X_{n_{K}},V_{n_{K}})\mapsto(x_{1},(\nu_{i},\tau_{i})_{i\leq n_{K}-1},V_{n_{K}})\end{array}

is a diffeomosphism and

(6.2)

\int\mu^{n_{K}-1}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left(Z_{n_{K}}\right)g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}\\
=\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\\
\times M^{\otimes n_{K}}\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}.

Definition 6.1

(Pseudotrajectories for punctual particles)
.

Fix a collision tree
T:=(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},\bar{s}_{i})
and collision parameters
(V_{n_{k}},\tau_{[n_{k}-1]},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})
. We now define the pseudotrajectories for punctual particles. The velocities
{\mathsf{V}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(\tau,T)
follow a jump process: at time
0
,
{\mathsf{V}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(\tau=0,T)=V_{n_{K}}
. At time
\tau_{i}
, if
\bar{s}_{i}=1
the velocities of particles
q_{i}
,
\bar{q}_{i}
jump to
v_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+})
,
v_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+})
given by
(v_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+}),v_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+}),\tilde{\nu}_{i}):=\xi_{\alpha}(v_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-}),v_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-}),{\nu}_{i})
(
\xi_{\alpha}
the scattering map defined in Definition
2.1
).

We defined
\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}
the set of the
(x_{1},(\nu_{i})_{i\leq n_{K}-1},V_{n_{K}})
such that for all
j
,
\left({\mathsf{v}}^{0}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{0}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right)\cdot\nu_{i}
is positive. Note that
\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}\subset\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}
.

We have formally the convergence

\int\mu^{n_{K}-1}\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\left(Z_{n_{K}}\right)g_{n_{K}}(Z_{n_{K}})M^{\otimes n_{K}}dZ_{n_{K}}\\
\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\\
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}.

In order to have explicit rates of convergence, we decompose the error into two parts:

(6.3)

\int\mu^{n_{K}-1}\,\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]g_{n_{K}}^{\varepsilon}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}\\
=\eqref{eq:R1}+\eqref{eq:R2}+\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\\
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}},

where we define

(6.4)

=\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}}\Big(h({\mathsf{z}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{n_{K}}(0,T))-h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\Big)\\
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}},

(6.5)

=-\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{T}^{0}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{G}_{T}^{\varepsilon}}\right)\\
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}},

The error parts are estimated using the following standard results:

Lemma 6.1
.

Fix
\bar{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})
and denote for
p\in[1,2]

\Lambda_{T}^{p}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}):=\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k}-1}\left|{\mathsf{v}}^{0}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{0}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|^{p}

For any
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, we have

(6.6)

\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\Lambda_{T}^{p}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\\
\leq C^{n_{K}}t^{n_{K-1}-1}\theta^{n_{K}-n_{K-1}}.

Proof.

Fix first the collision tree
T:=(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},\bar{s}_{i})_{i}
. We sum on each
\nu_{i}
in the decreasing order:

(6.7)

\sum_{(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},\bar{s}_{i})}\int\left|{\mathsf{v}}^{0}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}^{0}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{i}\leq C\sum_{(\bar{q}_{i},\bar{s}_{i})}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})\right|^{p}\leq Cn_{K}^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\left|{\mathsf{V}}_{n_{K}}(\tau_{i})\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}\\
\leq Cn_{K}^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\left|V_{n_{K}}\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}

using the conservation of energy.

Hence,

\sum_{T}\int\Lambda^{p}_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}M^{\otimes n_{K}}dV_{n_{K}}\leq C^{n_{K}}n_{K}^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\int\left|V_{n_{K}}\right|^{\frac{n_{K}p}{2}}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\\
\leq{C}_{1}^{n_{K}}n_{K}^{2n_{K}}\int e^{-\frac{1}{4}|V_{n_{K}}|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\leq{C}_{2}^{n_{K}}n_{K}^{2n_{K}}.

Integrating the collision times

\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\leq\prod_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{\theta^{n_{k}-n_{k+1}}}{(n_{k}-n_{k+1})!}\leq\frac{((K-1)\theta)^{n_{K-1}-1}}{(n_{K-1}-1)!}\frac{\theta^{n_{K}-n_{K+1}}}{(n_{K}-n_{K+1})!}\\
\leq\frac{2^{n_{K}-1}t^{n_{K-1}-1}\theta^{n_{K}-n_{K+1}}}{(n_{K}-1)!}.

Finally, we multiply the two previous inequalities and
\tfrac{1}{(n_{K}-1)!}
. Using the Stirling formula, we obtain the expected estimation.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Fix
\bar{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{K})
. There exists a constant
\mathfrak{a}\in(0,1))
, depending only on the dimension, such that
for any
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, we have

(6.8)

\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\left|1-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{G}_{T}^{\varepsilon}}\right|\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\penalty\ \,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\\
\leq C^{n_{K}}t^{n_{K}+10}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}.
