This is an estimation of the set of parameters leading to a pathology (a recollision, a triple interaction, or an overlap). It is proven in Annex
B.2
. From Lemma
6.2
we deduce

|\eqref{eq:R2}|\leq C(Ct)^{n_{K}}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\|g\|\,\|h\|.

Lemma 6.3
.

Fix
\bar{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})
,
T
,
\varepsilon>0
and
(x_{1},(\tau_{i},\nu_{i})_{i},V_{n_{K}})\in\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}
. We have

(6.9)

\forall\tau\in[0,t],\penalty\ \big|{\mathsf{X}}^{\varepsilon}_{n_{K}}(\tau)-{\mathsf{X}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(\tau)\big|\leq 2n_{K}\mathbb{V}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{K}-1}\frac{\varepsilon\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i}))\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\;.

Proof.

Thanks to the estimation of the interaction time (
B.1
), the
i
-th collision lasts at most a time
\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i}))\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}

. Hence, the two trajectories
{\mathsf{X}}^{\varepsilon}_{n_{K}}(\tau)
and
{\mathsf{X}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(\tau)
have coincident velocities for
\tau
outside the union of the interval

\bigcup_{i=1}^{n_{K}-1}\left[\tau_{i},\tau_{i}+\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i}))\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\right].

During a collision, a particle can cross a distance smaller than
\tfrac{\varepsilon\mathbb{V}\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i}))\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}
which bounds the error that a collision creates. Hence, after
n_{K}
collisions, summing over all the possible particles, we obtain the expected bound.

∎

Lemma 6.4
.

Fix
\bar{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{K})
. For any
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small , we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:R1}|\leq C(Ct)^{n_{K}-1}\mathbb{V}\varepsilon^{1/2}\|g\|_{1}\|h\|_{1}.

Proof.

We have forbid any recollision, multiple encounter
, and overlap. Hence, the velocities of pseudotrajectories of particles of sizes
\varepsilon
and
0
coincide. Using the inequality
|f(z)-f(z^{\prime})|\leq(1\wedge|z|)\|f\|_{1}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eqref{eq:R1}\leq\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}}2n_{K}\mathbb{V}\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{K}-1}1\wedge&\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|\left({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\\
\times\Lambda&(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\end{split}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{-n_{K}+1}\mathbb{V}\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}}{(n_{K}!)^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n_{K}-1\\[3.0pt]
T\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}}1\wedge\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|\left({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}.

We need to bound

(6.11)

\sum_{T}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}}1\wedge\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}{\left|\left({\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\rm v}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}

Note that
{\rm v}^{0}_{q}(\tau_{i}^{+})
does not depend on the
\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}
, but only on the order of the collisions.

Fix a collision tree
T=(q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i},s_{i})_{i}
. We define for
i\in[1,n_{K}-1]
the applications
\left(\Xi_{T}^{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n_{K}}
as
\Xi_{T}^{i}=\text{id}
if
i=1
, and

(6.12)

\Xi_{T}^{i}:(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\mapsto\left\{\begin{split}&\Big(v_{1},\cdots,\overset{q_{i-1}}{v^{\prime}_{q_{i-1}}},\cdots,\overset{q^{\prime}_{i-1}}{v^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}_{i-1}}},\cdots,v_{n_{K}},\nu_{1},\cdots,\overset{i-1}{\nu^{\prime}_{i-1}},\cdots,\nu_{n_{K}-1}\Big)\penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ s_{i}=1\\
&\Big(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{1},\cdots,\overset{i-1}{-\nu_{i-1}},\cdots,\nu_{n_{K}-1}\Big)\penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ s_{i}=1\end{split}\right.

with the new velocities given by the scattering
(v^{\prime}_{q_{i-1}},v^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}_{i-1}},\nu^{\prime}_{i-1}):=\xi_{\alpha}((v^{\prime}_{q_{i-1}},v^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}_{i-1}},\nu^{\prime}_{i-1}))
.
We have that

({\rm V}_{n_{K}}(\tau_{i}^{-}),\nu^{i}_{[n_{K}-1]}):=\Xi_{T}^{i}\Xi_{T}^{i-1}\cdots\Xi_{T}^{1}({V}_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}).

Using that the Jacobian of the scattering
\xi_{\alpha}
is
1
and the conservation by the scattering of the energy and angular momentum, the Jacobian of the transformation
\Xi_{T}^{i}\Xi_{T}^{i-1}\cdots\Xi_{T}^{1}
is

\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\to\Lambda_{T}^{(i)}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}

where we start now the velocity process at time
\tau_{i}^{-}
with
{\rm V}_{n_{K}}(\tau_{i}^{-}):=V_{n_{K}}
and

\Lambda_{T}^{(i)}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}):=\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\left(\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{+})\right)\cdot\nu_{j}\right)_{+}\prod_{j=i}^{n_{k}-1}\left(\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})\right)\cdot\nu_{j}\right)_{-}.

Hence,

\begin{split}\eqref{eq:R4}\leq\sum_{T}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}}1\wedge\frac{\varepsilon|v_{q_{i}}-{v}_{q_{i}}|}{\left|\left(v_{q_{i}}-{v}_{q_{i}}\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\Lambda_{T}^{(i)}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}.\end{split}

Using the usual bound on
\sum_{T}\Lambda(V_{n_{K}},\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})
that can be adapted to
\Lambda_{i}
, and that for
\vec{e}_{1}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}1\wedge\tfrac{\delta}{|\vec{e}_{1}\times\sigma|^{2}}d\sigma\lesssim\int_{0}^{\pi}\left(1\wedge\tfrac{\delta}{|\sin\theta|^{2}}\right)\sin^{d-2}\theta d\theta\lesssim\delta^{1/2},

one has

\eqref{eq:R4}\leq\sum_{(q,q^{\prime})}\int(Cn_{K}|V_{n_{K}}|^{2}+1)^{n_{K}}1\wedge\tfrac{\varepsilon|v_{q}-{v}_{q^{\prime}}|}{\left|\left(v_{q}-{v}_{q^{\prime}}\right)\times\nu\right|^{2}}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\\
\leq n_{K}^{2}(C^{\prime}n^{2}_{K})^{n_{K}}\int\frac{\varepsilon^{1/2}e^{-\frac{|V_{n_{K}}|^{2}}{4}}\,\mathrm{d}{V_{n_{K}}}}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{1/2}}\leq\varepsilon^{1/2}(C^{\prime\prime}n^{2}_{K})^{n_{K}}

as
\tfrac{1}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{1/2}}
is an integrable singularity. This concludes the proof.

∎

Finally, we get for any
h
and
g
Lipschitz

\int\mu_{\varepsilon}^{n_{K}-1}\,\Psi^{0,t}_{\underline{n}_{K}}[h]\,g_{n_{K}}^{\varepsilon}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{n_{K}}=\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\\
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\penalty\ \,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\\
+O\bigg(\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{K}}\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\bigg).

and therefore

(6.13)

G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{main}}(t)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{K}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{K}+1}}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\\[0.0pt]
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{K}-1]}\penalty\ \,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}+\,O\left(\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\right).

6.2.
Linearized Boltzmann equation

We identify now the main part of (
6.13
).

Let
{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}
be the solution of the linearized Boltzmann equation

\begin{split}\partial_{t}{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t)&=\frac{1}{\mathfrak{d}}\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t),\\
{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t=0)&=g\end{split}

where
\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}
is the linearized Boltzmann operator associated to the potential
\alpha\mathscr{V}(\cdot)

\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}g(v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\big(g(v^{\prime})+g(v_{*}^{\prime})-g(v)-g(v_{*})\big)M(v_{*})\big((v-v_{*})\cdot\nu\big)_{+}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\,\,\mathrm{d}v_{*}.

This equation can be rewritten in the Duhamel form:

{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t)=S(t)g+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{d}}\int_{0}^{t}S(t-\tau_{1})\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(\tau_{1})\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}

where
S(\tau)
is the free transport

S(\tau)g(x,v)=g(x-tv,v).

We iterate this formula, but we still want to cut the cases with too many collisions in a short time interval (as for the particle system). Let’s define

Q_{m,n}(\tau)[g]=\frac{1}{\mathfrak{d}^{m-n}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{n}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}\cdots\int_{0}^{t_{m+2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{m+1}S(t-\tau_{n})\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}S(\tau_{n}-\tau_{n-1})\cdots\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}S(\tau_{m+1})g,

and for
\underline{n}_{k}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})
with
1\leq n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k}
,

Q_{\underline{n}_{k}}(\tau)g=Q_{1,n_{1}}(\tfrac{\tau}{k})Q_{n_{1},n_{2}}(\tfrac{\tau}{k})\cdots Q_{n_{k-1},n_{k}}(\tfrac{\tau}{k})[g].

We have

(6.14)

\begin{split}{\mathbf{g}}(t)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{K}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}Q_{\underline{n}_{k}}(t)[g]+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k-1}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}>+n_{k-1}+2^{k}}Q_{\underline{n}_{k}}(k\theta)[{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t-k\theta)].\end{split}

In a first time, we bound the term of the sum: we have the classical estimates

Proposition 6.5
.

There exists a constant
C
such that for any
g\in L^{2}(M(v)dz)
, and
\underline{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})
,

(6.15)

\big\|Q_{\underline{n}}(k\theta)g\big\|_{L^{2}(M^{2}(v)dz)}\leq\big(\tfrac{C(k-1)\theta}{\mathfrak{d}}\big)^{\frac{n_{k-1}}{2}}\big(\tfrac{C\theta}{\mathfrak{d}}\big)^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}\,\|g\|_{L^{2}(M(v)dz)}.

The proof is the same as the one of Proposition 7.5 of
[
LB25
]
.

Because
{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t)
is bounded in
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}(M(v)dz)
by
\|g\|_{L^{2}(M(v)dz)}\leq C\|g\|_{0}
, we can bound the rest term of (
6.14
) by

(6.16)

\begin{split}\Bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k-1}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}&\sum_{n_{k}>+n_{k-1}+2^{k}}\int h(z)Q_{\underline{n}_{k}}(k\theta)[{\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t-k\theta)](z)M(v)\,\mathrm{d}z\Bigg|\\
&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k-1}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}>+n_{k-1}+2^{k}}\big(\tfrac{C(k-1)\theta}{\mathfrak{d}}\big)^{\frac{n_{k-1}}{2}}\big(\tfrac{C\theta}{\mathfrak{d}}\big)^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}\,\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\\
&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k-1}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k}>2^{k}+n_{k-1}}\left(C\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{n_{k}-n_{k-1}}{2}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\\
&\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{K}2^{k^{2}}\left(C\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}\right)^{2^{k-1}}\|g\|\|h\|\leq C\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}.\end{split}

The series converges since
\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}<1
.

The final step is the identification of the main part in (
6.14
):

Proposition 6.6
.

Fix
\underline{n}_{K}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{K})
an increasing sequence of integer. Then

(6.17)

\int_{\mathbb{D}}h(z)Q_{\underline{n}_{k}}(t)[g](z)M(v)dz=\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-n_{k}+1}}{(n_{k}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{K}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)g_{n_{k}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{k}}(0,T))\\
\times\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k}}.

Remark 6.1
.

It is the counterpart of the Step 2 of the proof of Proposition IV.1 of
[
BGSRS22
]
.

Proof.

We fix for the moment the collision times
(\tau_{i})_{i}
.

Definition 6.2
.

Fix a collision tree
T:=(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},\bar{s}_{i})_{i\leq n_{k}-1)}
and a final particle
q_{f}
. We say that a sequence
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{\ell})
is
causal
if

i_{1}<\cdots<i_{\ell},\penalty\ \forall j<\ell,\penalty\ \{q_{i_{j}},\bar{q}_{i_{j}}\}\cap\{q_{i_{j+1}},\bar{q}_{i_{j+1}}\}\neq\emptyset.

A particle
\bar{q}

influences
the particle
1
(respectively
q_{f}
) if there exists a causal sequence
(i_{1},\cdots,i_{\ell})
such that
\bar{q}\in\{q_{i_{1}},\bar{q}_{i_{1}}\}
and
1\in\{q_{i_{\ell}},\bar{q}_{i_{\ell}}\}
(respectively
q_{f}\in\{q_{i_{1}},\bar{q}_{i_{1}}\}
and
\bar{q}\in\{q_{i_{\ell}},\bar{q}_{i_{\ell}}\}
).

We begin by developing
g_{n_{K}}
:

(6.18)

\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathbb{G}_{T}^{0}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T))g_{n_{k}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{k}}(0,T))\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k}}\\
=\sum_{q_{f}=1}^{n_{k}}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathbb{G}_{T}^{0}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T))g({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{q_{f}}(0,T))\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k}}.

Suppose that there exists a particle
\bar{q}
that does not influence
1
. If
\bar{q}
has more than two neighbor particles, one of them is farther from the particle
1
in the graph
T
(the graph
T
is a tree). Then, this new particle does not influence
1
. We deduce that without loss of generality, we can suppose that
q
has only one neighbor. Then, it has only one collision
\bar{\iota}
.

We now use the application
\Xi_{T}^{i}
defined in (
6.12
). We recall that

\Xi_{T}^{\bar{\iota}}\Xi_{T}^{\bar{\iota}-1}\cdots\Xi_{T}^{1}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})=(\tilde{V}_{n_{K}}={\mathsf{V}}_{n_{K}}(\tau_{\bar{\iota}}^{-}),\tilde{\nu}_{[n_{K}-1]}).

In a second time, for a fixed
(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})
, we perform the translation
x_{1}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{1}:={\mathsf{x}}_{1}(\tau_{\bar{\iota}})
.
The Jacobian of
\tau_{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}(\tau_{i})}\Xi_{T}^{\bar{\iota}}\Xi_{T}^{\bar{\iota}-1}\cdots\Xi_{T}^{1}
is

\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}{V}_{n_{K}}\to\Lambda_{T}^{(i)}(\tilde{V}_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{V}_{n_{K}}.

We start now the velocity process at time
\tau_{i}^{-}
with
{\rm V}_{n_{K}}(\tau_{i}^{-}):=\tilde{V}_{n_{K}}
and

\Lambda_{T}^{(i)}(\tilde{V}_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}):=\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\left(\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{+})\right)\cdot\nu_{j}\right)_{+}\prod_{j=i}^{n_{k}-1}\left(\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})\right)\cdot\nu_{j}\right)_{-}.

We pair
T
with the tree
\tilde{T}
as

\tilde{T}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(q_{j},\bar{q}_{j},\bar{s}_{j})&\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ j\neq i\\
(q_{j},\bar{q}_{j},-\bar{s}_{j})&\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ j=i.\end{array}\right.

Then
\sigma(T)=-\sigma(\tilde{T})
, and for same
(\tilde{V}_{n_{K}},\tau_{[n_{K}-1]},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})
, we have
{\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T)={\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,\tilde{T})
and
{\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{q}(0,T)={\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{q}(0,\tilde{T})
. We have
\Lambda_{T}^{(i)}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})=\Lambda_{\tilde{T}}^{(i)}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})
. Thus

\sigma(T)\int_{\mathbb{G}_{T}^{0}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T))g({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{q}(0,T))\Lambda(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\\
=-\sigma(\tilde{T})\int_{\mathbb{G}_{\tilde{T}}^{0}}h({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,\tilde{T}))g({\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{q}(0,\tilde{T}))\Lambda(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}.

The same strategy can be reproduced if there exists some particle that does not influence the final particle
q_{f}
. Hence, it remains only in (
6.18
) the trees such that every particle influences both
1
and
q_{f}
. The other terms are exactly compensated.

For a remaining tree
T=(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},\nu_{i})_{i}
we can prove the following lemma:

Lemma 6.7
.

For all
i
, the set
\{q_{i},\bar{q}_{i}\}\cap\{q_{i+1},\bar{q}_{i+1}\}
has exactly one element.

Proof.

First, there exists a causal sequence
(q_{i_{1}},\bar{q}_{i_{1}}),\cdots,(q_{i_{k}},\bar{q}_{i_{k}})
such that
q_{f}\in\{q_{i_{1}},\bar{q}_{i_{1}}\}
and
1\in\{q_{i_{k}},\bar{q}_{i_{k}}\}
.

Consider
q\notin\bigcup_{l}\{(q_{i_{k}},\bar{q}_{i_{k}})\}
. There exist two causal paths
(q_{j_{1}},\bar{q}_{j_{1}}),\cdots,(q_{j_{\ell}},\bar{q}_{j_{\ell}})
from
q_{f}
to
q
and
(q_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{1}}),\cdots,(q_{j^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}})
from
q
to
1
. We define
\ell_{0}
such that
\forall l\leq\ell_{0}
,
(q_{j_{\ell-l+1}},\bar{q}_{j_{\ell-l+1}})=(q_{j^{\prime}_{l}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{l}})
. The sequence
(q_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{1}}),\cdots,(q_{j^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}}})
is both increasing and decreasing, thus
\ell_{0}\leq 1
. The sequence
(q_{j^{\prime}_{1}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{1}}),\cdots,(q_{j_{\ell-\ell_{0}}},\bar{q}_{j_{\ell-\ell_{0}}}),(q_{j^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}+1}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}+1}}),\cdots,(q_{j^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}},\bar{q}_{j^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}})
is a causal path from
q_{f}
to
1
. We deduce that
\ell_{0}=1
.

Finally, for any
l\leq k-1
,
j\in]i_{l},i_{l+1}[
, one
\{q_{j},\bar{q}_{j}\}\cap\{q_{i_{l}},\bar{q}_{i_{l}}\}\cap\{q_{i_{l+1}},\bar{q}_{i_{l+1}}\}
. The conclusion follows
∎

Using the lemma, we can construct the sequences
(\tilde{q}_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n_{K}-1}
,
(\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n_{K}-1}
and
(\tilde{s}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n_{K}-1}
by

\tilde{q}_{0}:=q_{f},\penalty\ \tilde{q}_{n_{K}-1}:=1,\penalty\ \{\tilde{q}_{i}\}:=\{q_{i},\bar{q}_{i}\}\cap\{q_{i+1},\bar{q}_{i+1}\},\penalty\ \{\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{i}\}:=\{q_{i},\bar{q}_{i}\}\setminus\{\tilde{q}_{i}\}

and

\tilde{s}_{i}:=\left\{\begin{split}&\,1\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ q_{i}=q_{i-1}\\
&-1\penalty\ \text{else}.\end{split}\right.

The sequence
(\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{i})_{i}
encodes the order in which particles collide. In addition, we can reconstruct
T
for a given sequence
(\bar{s}_{i},\tilde{s}_{i},\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime})_{i}
.

We reorder the particles such that
\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{i}=n_{K}-i
(there are
(n_{K}-1)!
possibilities).

Finally, we have to identify the four possible
(\bar{s}_{i},\tilde{s}_{i})_{i}
with the four parts of
\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}
:
(1,1)
with
g(v^{\prime})
(we follow the same particle that is deviated by the collision),
(1,-1)
with
-g(v)
(we follow the same particle that is not deviated by the collision),
(-1,1)
with
g(v_{*}^{\prime})
and
(-1,-1)
with
-g(v_{*})
. There are
(n_{K}-1)!
possible sequences
(\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime})_{i}
.

We conclude that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{(n_{K}-1)!}\sum_{T}\sigma(T)\int_{\mathbb{G}_{T}^{0}}h(&{\mathsf{z}}^{0}_{1}(t,T))g_{n_{K}}({\mathsf{Z}}^{0}_{n_{K}}(0,T))\Lambda(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{[n_{K}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}M^{\otimes n_{K}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{K}}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{D}}h(z)S(t-\tau_{n_{K}-1})\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}S(\tau_{n_{K}-1}-\tau_{n_{K}-2})\cdots\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}S(\tau_{1})g(z)\penalty\ M(v)\,\mathrm{d}z.\end{split}

We obtain the expected result by integrating with respect to
(\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{n_{K}-1})
.
∎

Combining the preceding proposition and the estimations (
6.16
) and (
6.13
), we obtain:

(6.19)

G^{\rm main}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{D}}h(z){\mathbf{g}}_{\alpha}(t,z)M(z)\,\mathrm{d}z+O\left(\left(\tfrac{\theta t}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}K2^{K^{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\right)\|h\|_{1}\|g\|_{1}\right).

7.
Estimation of non-pathological recollisions

In the last two sections, we estimate the error terms where the pseudotrajectory can have a recollision. We begin with the case of non-pathological recollision.

G^{\text{rec},1}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq K-1\\
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq k}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k+2}\geq n_{k+2}\geq n_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}\Psi^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k}}}(t_{s})\right)\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\right]

where
t-t_{s}=k\theta+k^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}
.

Proposition 7.1
.

There exists a constant
\mathfrak{a}\in(0,1))
, depending only on the dimension, such that
for
\varepsilon
small enough,

(7.1)

\begin{split}\left|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},1}(t)\right|&\leq\|g\|\|h\|\varepsilon^{{\mathfrak{a}}/2}(C^{\prime}t)^{2^{t/\theta}+d+9}.\end{split}

It is sufficient to prove the two following estimations:

Proposition 7.2
.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\underline{n}:=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k+2})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
. Then fixing
x_{1}=0
we have

(7.2)

\int\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|{\Psi}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}[h](\tr_{y}Z_{n_{k+2}})\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{k}d}{d}}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}\\
\leq\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}C^{n_{k+2}}\delta^{2}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}t^{n_{k}+9+d}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}},

and, for
m\in[1,n_{k+2}]
,

(7.3)

\int\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|{\Psi}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}[h]\circledast_{l}{\Psi}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}[h](\tr_{y}Z_{2n_{k+2}-m})\big|M^{\otimes(2n_{k+2}-m)}\,\mathrm{d}V_{2n_{n+2}-m}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,2n_{k+2}-m}\\
\leq\frac{\mu^{m-1}}{n_{k+2}^{m}}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}C^{n_{k+2}}\right)^{2}\delta^{2}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}t^{n_{k}+n_{k+2}+9+d}.

Using these estimations and Corollary
4.3
,

\begin{split}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[&\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}{\Psi}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{\underline{i}_{n_{k+2}}}(t_{s})\right)\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
&\leq\|h\|_{0}\|g\|_{0}\frac{C^{n_{k+2}}}{\mathfrak{d}^{n_{k+2}-1}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+{\mathfrak{a}}}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}\delta^{2}t^{n_{k}+d+9}+\left(e^{{\mathfrak{a}}}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}\delta^{2}t^{n_{k}+d+9+m}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}C^{n_{k+2}}(\tfrac{\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{\frac{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}{2}}(\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{\frac{n_{k+2}+n_{k}}{2}+d+9}\\[3.0pt]
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}+d+9}(\tfrac{Ct\theta}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}})^{\frac{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}{2}}.\end{split}

Using that
\tfrac{Ct\theta}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}<1
and
K^{\prime}\delta=\theta\leq 1
, we can sum on
k
,
k^{\prime}
and
\underline{n}_{k+2}

\begin{split}\left|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},1}(t)\right|&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq K-1\\
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{k+2}\geq n_{k+1}\geq n_{k}\end{subarray}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}+d+9}(\tfrac{Ct\theta}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}})^{\frac{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}{2}}\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}K^{\prime}\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}K^{K^{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K}+d+9}\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{2}}(\tfrac{C^{\prime}t}{2\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K}+d+9}\end{split}

This concludes the proof of (
7.1
).

Proof of (
7.2
).

We recall that the pseudotrajectory development takes the form

{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{>,t-t_{s}}[h]:=\frac{1}{(n_{k+2}-1)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\in\omega\subset[n_{k+2}]\\
|\omega|=n_{k+1}\\
(s_{i})_{i\leq n_{i+2}-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\prod_{k=1}^{n_{l}-1}s_{k}\,h({\mathsf{z}}_{q}(t,\cdot,\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(t-i\theta)=n_{i}}.

Here
\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}
is the set of initial configurations
Z_{n_{k+2}}
such that the pseudotrajectory has

•

1
the final particle at time
t-t_{s}
,

•

\omega
the set of particles at time
\delta
,

•

at least one recollision,

•

no pathological recollision (thanks to the conditioning on
\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}
).

Lemma 7.3
.

There exists a constant
{\mathfrak{a}}\in(0,1)
such that for any
\underline{n}
,
k^{\prime}
and
(s_{i})_{i}
,

(7.4)

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}M^{\otimes n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k+2}}\leq C^{\prime}\left(\frac{C^{\prime}n_{k+2}}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{n_{k+2}-1}\delta^{2}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}t^{n_{k}+2d+4}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}.

Proof.

We may define the clustering tree
T^{>}
as before, by looking at collisions in temporal order and keeping only the clustering collisions.
However, this will not be sufficient to characterize the initial data.

Let
(\bar{q},\bar{q}^{\prime})
(with
\bar{q}<\bar{q}^{\prime}
) be the first two particles having a non-clustering collision,
\tau_{\rm cycle}
the time of this collision, and
c\in[1,n_{k+2}-1]
such that
\tau_{\rm cycle}
lies between the times of the
c
-th and the
(c+1)
-th clustering collision. The parameters
\bar{T}:=(T^{>},(\bar{q},\bar{q}^{\prime},c))
provide a partition of the set of initial data.

We denote

\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}(\tau_{i})_{i\leq n_{k+2}-1},\penalty\ \tau_{i}\leq\tau_{i+1},\\
\forall j\leq n_{k+2}-n_{k+1},\penalty\ \tau_{j}\leq\delta\\
\forall j\leq n_{k+2}-n_{k}[,\penalty\ \tau_{j}\leq k^{\prime}\delta\\
\forall\ell\leq k,\penalty\ j\leq n_{k+2}-n_{k+2-\ell-1},\penalty\ \tau_{j}\leq k^{\prime}\delta+(\ell+1)\theta\end{array}\right\}

For a given initial data
Z_{n}
and
\bar{T}:=(T^{>},(\bar{q},\bar{q}^{\prime},c))
, we define
\tau_{i}
as the time of the
i
-th clustering collision and
\nu_{i}:=({\rm x}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\rm x}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{\prime}))/\varepsilon
. We denote
\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{>,t-t_{s},\bar{T}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}
the image of the set of initial datum

\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}\cap\{T^{>}\text{ is the collision tree, first collision implies }(\bar{q},\bar{q}^{\prime})\text{ during }(\tau_{c},\tau_{c+1})\}

by the application
(X_{2,n_{k+2}},V_{n_{k+2}})\to(\tau_{[n_{k+2}-1]},\nu_{[n_{k+2}-1]},V_{n_{k+2}})
.

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{k+2}d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}dV_{n_{k+2}}\\
=\frac{1}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}\sum_{\bar{T}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{>,t-t_{s},\bar{T}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k+2}-1}|({\rm v}^{\varepsilon}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\rm v}^{\varepsilon}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}(\tau_{i}))\cdot\nu_{i}|\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tau_{i}}M^{\otimes n_{k+2}}dV_{n_{k+2}}.

Using Lemma
B.2
, we get

(7.5)

\begin{split}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{k+2}d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}dV_{n_{k+2}}\leq&\frac{(Cn_{k+2})^{n_{k+2}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}t^{n_{k}-1}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-1)_{+}}\varepsilon^{1/4}\\
\leq&\frac{(Cn_{k+2})^{n_{k+2}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}t^{n_{k}-1}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-1)_{+}}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{1/12}\end{split}

using that
\delta=\varepsilon^{1/12}
.
