Example of construction of a clustering tree.

∎

We obtain the expected result by summing on

(s_{i})_{i\leq n_{k+2}-1}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n_{k+2}-1},\penalty\ \omega\subset[n_{k+2}],\penalty\ q\in\omega

and dividing by
n_{k+2}!
.
∎

Proof of (
7.3
).

We use first the same bound as in the proof of (
7.2
) and of (
5.3
),

(7.6)

\left|{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{>,t-t_{s}}[h]\circledast_{m}{\Psi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{>,t-t_{s}}[h](Z_{2n_{k+2}-m})\right|\\
\leq\frac{\|h\|^{2}}{(n_{k+2}!)^{2}}\frac{(n_{k+2}-m)!^{2}m!}{n_{k+2}^{2}(2n_{k+2}-m)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{\omega}\cup\bar{\omega}^{\prime}=[2n_{k+2}-m]\\
|\bar{\omega}|=|\bar{\omega}^{\prime}|=n_{k+2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\omega\subset\bar{\omega}\\
|\omega|=n_{k+1}\\
(s_{i})_{i\leq n_{i+2}-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q^{\prime}\in\omega^{\prime}\subset\bar{\omega}^{\prime}\\
|\omega^{\prime}|=n_{k+1}\\
(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i\leq n_{i+2}-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}\!\!\!(Z_{\bar{\omega}})\penalty\ \penalty\ \\
\times\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(k^{\prime}\delta)=n_{k}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q^{\prime}\},\omega^{\prime},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}}\!\!\!(Z_{\bar{\omega}^{\prime}}).

where
\mathfrak{n}(\theta)
is the number of particles at time
\theta
in the pseudotrajectory
{\rm Z}(\tau)
.
Note that the formula is invariant under translation. We can then set
x_{1}=0
and integrate with respect to the other variables.

Using the same strategy as in the proof of (
5.3
), we have

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q^{\prime}\},\omega^{\prime},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}}\!\!\!(Z_{\bar{\omega}^{\prime}})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k+2}-m}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{\bar{\omega}^{\prime}\setminus\bar{\omega}}\leq C\left(\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{n_{k}-m}t^{n_{k+2}-m}(2n_{k+2}-m)^{n_{k+2}-m}.

The sum over the remaining particles is estimated using (
7.5
)

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q^{\prime}\},\omega^{\prime},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}}\!\!\!(Z_{\bar{\omega}^{\prime}})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{>,t-t_{s}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}(Z_{\omega})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k+2}-m}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{(2n_{k+2}-m)d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{1,2n_{k+2}-m}dV_{2n_{k+2}-m}\\
\leq\frac{(C2n_{k+2})^{2n_{k+2}-m}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{2n_{k+2}-m-1}}\delta^{2}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-3)_{+}}t^{n_{k}+n_{k+2}+9+d}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}.

We obtain the expected result by combining the two estimations, summing on the possible parameters
((s_{i})_{i},\bar{\omega},\omega,q)
and
((s^{\prime}_{i})_{i},\bar{\omega}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},q^{\prime})
and then dividing by
(n_{k+2})!^{2}
.
∎

8.
Estimation of the local recollisions

In the present section, we discuss
G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},2}(t)
defined by

G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},2}(t)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq K-1\\
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(n_{j})_{j\leq k}\\
0\leq n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{n_{k+2}\geq n_{k+1}\geq n_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\zeta_{\varepsilon}^{0}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\\[-10.0pt]
\times\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}\Psi^{,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}(t_{s})\right)\Bigg]

for
t_{s}:=t-k\theta-k^{\prime}\delta
.

We will prove the following bound:

Proposition 8.1
.

There exists a constant
\mathfrak{a}\in(0,1))
, depending only on the dimension, such that
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we have

(8.1)

\Big|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},2}(t)\Big|\leq C\|h\|_{0}\|g\|_{0}(C\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K+1}}\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}.

In the following, we denote

\begin{split}\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{n_{k+2}}):=\frac{1}{(n_{k+2})!}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n_{k+2}}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}\Psi^{,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left(Z_{\sigma[n_{k+2}]}\right)\end{split}

The aim of this part is to prove the following bound on
\Phi_{\underline{n}_{k+2},p}^{k^{\prime}}
:

Proposition 8.2
.

Fix
n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k+2}\leq p
. For
m\in\{1,\cdots p\}
we have for
x_{1}=0

(8.2)

\int\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(\tr_{y}Z_{n_{k+2}})\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{dn_{k+2}}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{2,n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}v_{1}\\
\leq\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}C^{n_{k+2}}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}t^{n_{k}-1},

(8.3)

\int\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}}\big|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}\circledast_{m}\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(\tr_{y}Z_{2n_{k+2}-m})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{2n_{k+2}-m}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d(2n_{k+2}-m)}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{2,2n_{k+2}-m}\,\mathrm{d}v_{1}\\
\leq\frac{\mu^{m-1}}{n_{k+2}^{m}}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}C^{n_{k+2}}\right)^{2}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}t^{n_{k}-1+n_{k+2}}.

Using the estimations (
8.2
) and (
8.3
), one obtains

\begin{array}[]{r@{}r@{}r}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon}\Bigg[\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}\Phi_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{{\underline{i}}_{n_{k+2}}}(t_{s}))\,\zeta^{0}_{\varepsilon}(g)\mathbbm{1}_{\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg]\Bigg)\Bigg|&\lx@intercol\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}C^{n_{k+2}}\Big((\tfrac{\delta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2}(\tfrac{\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}(\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}-1}\varepsilon^{\frac{2{\mathfrak{a}}+1}{2}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[-8.0pt]
&&+\Big((\tfrac{\delta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2}(\tfrac{\theta}{\mathfrak{d}})^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}(\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k+2}+n_{k}-1}\varepsilon^{{\mathfrak{a}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\\[8.0pt]
&\lx@intercol\leq\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}\|h\|_{0}\|g\|_{0}C^{n_{k+2}}(\tfrac{t^{2}\theta}{\mathfrak{d}^{3}})^{\frac{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}{2}}(\tfrac{t}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}},\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

as
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}/\mathfrak{d}\to 0
.

Using that
\tfrac{Ct\theta}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}}\leq 1
and
K^{\prime}\delta=\theta\leq 1
, we can sum on
k
,
k^{\prime}
and
\underline{n}_{k+2}

\begin{split}\left|G_{\varepsilon}^{\text{rec},2}(t)\right|&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq K-1\\
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\leq\cdots\leq n_{k}\\
n_{j}-n_{j-1}\leq 2^{j}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{k+2}\geq n_{k+1}\geq n_{k}\end{subarray}}\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{n_{k}}(\tfrac{Ct\theta}{\mathfrak{d}^{2}})^{\frac{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}{2}}\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}K^{\prime}\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{2}}K^{K^{2}}(\tfrac{Ct}{\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K}}\\
&\leq\|g\|_{0}\|h\|_{0}\varepsilon^{\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{2}}(\tfrac{C^{\prime}t}{2\mathfrak{d}})^{2^{K}}\end{split}

This concludes the proof of (
8.1
).

Proof of (
8.2
).

We recall that

\begin{split}\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{n_{k+2}}):=\frac{1}{(n_{k+2})!}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n_{k+2}}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}\Psi^{,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left(Z_{\sigma[n_{k+2}]}\right)\end{split}

In
\Psi^{0,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}[h]\left(Z_{[n_{k+2}]}\right)
we see three sets of indices:

•

1
the last particle,

•

[1,n_{k+1}]
the set of particles in "final" tree pseudotrajectory development,

•

[n_{k+1}+1,n_{k+2}]
the particles added in the first time interval.

Any permutation
\sigma
that sends
[1,n_{k+1}]
and
[n_{k+1}+1,n_{k+2}]
onto themselves stabilizes the function
\Psi^{0,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}[h]
. Hence,
\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2},p}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{p})
is equal to

\begin{split}\frac{(n_{k+1}-1)!(n_{k+2}-n_{k+1})!}{(n_{k+2})!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}=[n_{k+2}]\\
|\omega_{1}|=n_{k+1}\\
q_{1}\in\omega_{1}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{>,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}\Psi^{,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}[h]\left(z_{q_{1}},Z_{\omega_{1}\setminus\{q_{1}\}},Z_{\omega_{2}}\right).\end{split}

Let us develop
\Psi^{0,t-t_{s}-\delta}_{\underline{n}_{k+1}}\Phi^{0,\delta}_{n_{k+1},n_{k+2}}[h]
.
For
(s_{i})_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n_{k+1}-1}
,
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
a partition of
[n_{k+2}]
and
(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})
a partition of
[n_{k+2}]
with
\omega_{1}\subset\lambda_{1}
, we define the pseudotrajectory
\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}(\tau,Z_{n_{k+2}},q_{1},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},

(s_{i})_{i},(\lambda_{j})_{j})
by

•

for
\tau\leq\delta
,

\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}(\tau):={\mathsf{Z}}(\tau,Z_{\omega_{1}},(\lambda_{j})_{j})

•

for
\tau>\delta
, the particle of
\omega_{3}
are removed and

\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1}}(\tau):={\mathsf{Z}}(\tau-\delta,\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1}}(\delta),\{q_{1}\},(s_{i})_{i}).

Then
\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})
is equal to

\frac{1}{(n_{k+2})!}\penalty\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}=[n_{k+2}]\\
|\omega_{1}|=n_{k+1}\\
q_{1}\in\omega_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{(s_{i})_{i}}\Bigg(\sum_{{{\mathbf{l}}}=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ \lambda_{1}\supset\omega_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{\mathbf{l}})\\
\in\mathscr{P}^{{\mathbf{l}}-1}_{\omega_{2}\setminus\lambda_{1}}\end{subarray}}\Bigg)h\left(\bar{{\mathsf{z}}}_{\omega_{1}}(k\theta+k^{\prime}\delta)\right)\\
\times\mathbbm{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}(\cdot)\,{\rm has\,a}\\
{\rm pathology}\\
{\rm on\,}[0,\delta]\end{subarray}}\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k+2}-1}\!\!\!{s}_{i}\penalty\ \mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,k\theta+(k-1)\delta}_{\omega_{1},(s_{i})_{i}}}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{2}}(\delta))\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(t-i\theta)=n_{i}}\Bigg)\\
\times\Bigg(O\hskip-5.0ptO_{l}(Z_{\lambda_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}})\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda_{1}|}^{[m]}(Z_{\lambda_{1}})\prod_{i=2}^{{\mathbf{l}}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda_{i}|}(Z_{\lambda_{i}})\Bigg).

The functions
O\hskip-5.0ptO
and
\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta
are defined in Definitions
3.1
and
3.2
(in the definition, they are defined on a time interval
[0,t]
; here, they are defined on
[0,\delta]
).

The function
O\hskip-5.0ptO_{l}(Z_{\lambda_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}})
can be bounded by the Penrose’s tree inequality (see for example
[
BGSRS20
,
Jan
]
),

\left|O\hskip-5.0ptO_{l}(Z_{\lambda_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}})\right|=\left|\sum_{C\in\mathscr{C}([{\mathbf{l}}])}\prod_{(i,j)\in E(C)}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\lambda}_{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}{\lambda}_{j}}\right|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{l}}])}\prod_{(i,j)\in E(T)}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lambda}_{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}{\lambda}_{j}}.

The set
\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{l}}])
is the set of minimally connected graphs with vertices
[{\mathbf{l}}]
.

We explain now how to take advantage of the pathology of
\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}(\cdot)
.

Definition 8.1
.

For
r\geq 3
, we define the set
\mathscr{O}_{r}
as

(8.4)

\mathscr{O}_{r}:=\Big\{Z_{r}\in\mathbb{D}^{r},\penalty\ \exists(\varpi_{1},\cdots,\varpi_{{\mathbf{l}}}),\penalty\ {\rm the\penalty\ collision\penalty\ graph\penalty\ of\penalty\ }{\mathsf{Z}}_{r}(\cdot,Z_{z},(\varpi_{1},\cdots,\varpi_{{\mathbf{l}}}))\text{\penalty\ on\penalty\ }[0,\delta]\penalty\ {\rm is\penalty\ }\\
{\rm connected,\penalty\ and\penalty\ the\penalty\ pseudotrajectory\penalty\ has\penalty\ a\penalty\ pathology}\Big\}.

We recall that a pathology can be an overlap, a multiple encounter
, or a recollision (see Definition
3.7
).

For
r=2
, we define

(8.5)

\mathscr{O}_{2}:=\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leq\varepsilon\}\cup\{|(x_{1}-x_{2})+\delta(v_{1}-v_{2})|\leq\varepsilon\}.

Finally, for
\varpi\subset[n_{k+2}]
, the set
\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}
is defined as

(8.6)

\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}:=\left\{Z_{n_{k+2}}\in\mathbb{D}^{n_{k+2}},\penalty\ Z_{\varpi}\in\mathscr{O}_{|\varpi|}\right\}.

The
\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}
allows to control the recollision condition

\mathbbm{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}(\cdot)\,{\rm has\,a}\\
{\rm pathology}\\
{\rm on\,}[0,\delta]\end{subarray}}\leq\sum_{\varpi\subset[n_{k+2}]}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}.

This leads to the following bound on
\left|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})\right|
:

(8.7)

\frac{\|h\|_{0}}{(n_{k+2})!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}=[n_{k+2}]\\
|\omega_{1}|=n_{k+1}\\
q_{1}\in\omega_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\subset[n_{k+2}]\\
|\varpi|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\sum_{(s_{i})_{i}}\Bigg(\sum_{{{\mathbf{l}}}=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ \lambda_{1}\supset\omega_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{\mathbf{l}})\\
\in\mathscr{P}^{{\mathbf{l}}-1}_{\omega_{2}\setminus\lambda_{1}}\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,k\theta+(k-1)\delta}_{\{q_{1}\},(s_{i})_{i}}}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1}}(\delta))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(k^{\prime}\delta)=n_{k}}\\[-10.0pt]
\times\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{l}}])}\prod_{(i,j)\in E(T)}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lambda}_{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}{\lambda}_{j}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda_{1}|}^{[n_{k+1}]}(Z_{\lambda_{1}})\prod_{i=2}^{{\mathbf{l}}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda_{i}|}(Z_{\lambda_{i}})\Bigg).

Note that the right hand-side is invariant under translation. Thus, one can fix
x_{1}=0
and integrate with respect to the other variables.

We introduce a partition to control the pseudo-trajectory in the time interval
[0,\delta]
.

Definition 8.2

(Possible cluster partition)
.

Given
Z_{n_{k+2}}\in\mathbb{D}^{n_{k+2}}
, we construct the graph
G
with vertices
[n_{k+2}]
. The pair
(i,j)
is an edge of
G
if and only if there exists
\tilde{\omega}\subset[n_{k+2}]
and
(\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{\lambda}_{\ell})
a partition of
\tilde{\omega}
such that the collision graph of
{\rm Z}(\cdot,Z_{\tilde{\omega}},\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{\lambda}_{\ell})
on time interval
[0,\delta]
is connected. We introduce
\underline{\rho}:=(\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{{\mathbf{r}}})
the
possible cluster partition
as the set of the connected components of
G
.

We define
\mathscr{D}^{\underline{\rho}}_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{D}^{n_{k+2}}
as the set such that
\underline{\rho}
is the possible cluster partition. The
\big(\mathscr{D}^{\underline{\rho}}_{\varepsilon})_{\underline{\rho}}
form a partition of
\mathbb{D}^{n_{k+2}}
.

By definition of the potential cluster, a particle cannot interact with a particle of an other cluster for any time in
[0,\delta]
. Thus the systems
\rho^{i}
are isolated on
[0,\delta]
and all the dynamics in
[0,\delta]
are encoded inside the
(\rho_{i})
.

The parametrization of the pseudotrajectories is changed to a more adapted one. There exists a
\rho_{i}
containing
\varpi
. With a little loss of symmetry, one can suppose that it is
\rho_{1}
. In the same way, for any
\lambda_{j}
with
j\neq 1
there exists some
\rho_{i}
containing
\lambda_{j}
. For any
\rho_{i}

•

\underline{\omega}^{i}:=(\omega_{1}^{i},\omega_{2}^{i})
the partition of
\rho_{i}
defined by
\omega^{i}_{j}:=\omega_{j}\cap\rho_{i}
, note that the set
\omega^{i}_{1}
cannot be empty,

•

\underline{\lambda}^{i}:=\{\lambda^{i}_{1}:=\lambda_{1}\cap\rho_{i}\}\cup\{\lambda_{j}\text{\penalty\ for\penalty\ }j\geq 2\text{\penalty\ with\penalty\ }\lambda_{j}\subset\rho_{i}\}
a partition of
\rho_{i}
,

•

for
i\geq 1
,
\mathfrak{p}_{i}:=(\underline{\omega}^{i},\underline{\lambda}^{i})
,

•

\mathfrak{p}_{1}:=(\underline{\omega}^{1},\underline{\lambda}^{1},\varpi)
.

The set of possible
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
is denoted
\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})
. Because
\rho_{i}
is of size at most
\gamma
, there exists a constant
C_{\gamma}
depending only on
\gamma
such that
|\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})|\leq C_{\gamma}
. For a fix partition
\underline{\rho}
, the map
(\underline{\omega},{\varpi},\underline{\lambda})\mapsto(\mathfrak{p}_{i})_{i}
is onto.

The possible cluster partition also contains the overlap: if we denote two dynamical clusters
\lambda_{j}
and
\lambda_{j^{\prime}}
with
j,j^{\prime}\geq 2
, there exists a
\rho_{i}
containing both, and if
\lambda_{j}\subset\rho_{i}
has an overlap with
\lambda_{1}
, then
\lambda_{j}
has an overlap with
\lambda_{1}^{i}
. This last property allows us to rewrite the overlap cumulant: for any
Z_{n_{k+2}}
in
\mathscr{D}^{\underline{\rho}}_{\varepsilon}
,

\Big|\psi_{{\mathbf{l}}}\big(Z_{\lambda_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}}\big)\Big|\leq\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{l}}])}\prod_{(i,j)\in E(T)}\mathbbm{1}_{\lambda_{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}\lambda_{j}}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\sum_{T_{i}\in\mathscr{T}([|\underline{\lambda^{i}}|])}\prod_{(j,j^{\prime})\in E(T_{i})}\mathbbm{1}_{\lambda_{j}^{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}\lambda^{i}_{j^{\prime}}}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{l}}}\Big|\mathscr{T}([|\underline{\lambda^{i}}|])\Big|.

The right-hand side is bounded using that

\Big|\mathscr{T}([|\underline{\lambda^{i}}|])\Big|\leq|\underline{\lambda^{i}}|^{|\underline{\lambda^{i}}|-2}\leq|\rho_{i}|^{|\rho_{i}|}

(see section 2 of
[
BGSRS20
]
). As the symmetric conditioning imposes that
|\rho_{i}|\leq\gamma
, the cumulants
\Big|\psi_{{\mathbf{l}}}\big(Z_{\lambda_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}}\big)\Big|
are smaller than
(\gamma^{\gamma})^{n_{k+2}}
.

We now have the following bound

(8.8)

\big|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2},p}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{p})\big|\leq\frac{\gamma^{n_{k+2}}\|h\|_{0}}{(n_{k+2})!}\sum_{q_{1}=1}^{n_{k+2}}\sum_{{{\mathbf{r}}}=1}^{n_{k+2}}\sum_{\underline{\rho}\in\mathscr{P}^{{\mathbf{r}}}_{n_{k+2}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s_{i})_{i}\\
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}\in\underset{i}{\prod}\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})\end{subarray}}\penalty\ \mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{q_{1},(s_{i})_{i}}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}}),

where we denote

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(Z_{\rho_{1}}):=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}\mathbbm{1}_{\!\!\!\!\tiny\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{\rho_{1}}\text{\,form\,a\,possible\,cluster}\end{array}}\!\!,

\displaystyle\forall i\geq 2,\penalty\ \Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}}):=\mathbbm{1}_{\!\!\!\!\tiny\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{\rho_{i}}\text{\,form\,a\,possible\,cluster}\end{array}}\penalty\ {\rm and}

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{q_{1},(s_{i})_{i}}:=\Big\{Z_{p}\in\mathscr{D}^{\underline{\rho}}_{\varepsilon},\,\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1}}(\delta)\in\mathscr{R}^{0,k\theta+(k-1)\delta}_{\{q_{1}\},(s_{i})_{i}}\Big\}.

The same method as in
[
BGSRS23a
]
is used to control the condition
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{((s_{i})_{i}}}
.

For a pseudotrajectory
\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{n_{k+2}}(\tau)
, consider its collision graph
\mathscr{G}_{\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2}}^{[0,t-t_{s}]}
. Then, we can construct the graph
G
by identifying in
\mathscr{G}_{\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2}}^{[0,t-t_{s}]}
the particles in the same cluster
\rho_{i}
. Finally we can construct the
clustering trees

T^{>}:=(\nu_{i},\bar{\nu}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq{\mathbf{r}}-1}
where the
i
-th clustering collision in
G
happens between cluster
\rho_{\nu_{i}}
and
\rho_{\bar{\nu_{i}}}
.

Figure 10.
Example of construction of the clustering stets.

We need to count the number of clustering collisions of
T^{>}
happening between time
\delta
and time
k^{\prime}\delta
. If
{\mathbf{r}}>n_{k}
, all the
{\mathbf{r}}-1
collisions in
T^{>}
cannot correspond to the
n_{k}-1
collisions of the time interval
[k^{\prime}\delta,\theta]
. Thus, at least
({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}
collisions happen during
[\delta,k^{\prime}\delta]\subset[0,2\theta]
.

One needs a different representation of collision graphs. Let
L_{0}
be equal to
\{\{1\},\cdots,

\{{\mathbf{r}}\}\}
. The
L_{i}
and
(\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)})
are constructed sequentially: suppose that
L_{i-1}=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{l})
, the
(c_{j})
forming a partition of
[1,r]
. The
i
-th collision happens between cluster
\nu_{i}\in c_{a}
and
\bar{\nu}_{i}\in c_{b}
. Then:

•

L_{i}:=\big(L_{i-1}\setminus\{c_{a},c_{b}\}\big)\cup\{c_{a}\cup c_{b}\}
,

•

\{\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)}\}:=\{c_{a},c_{b}\}
with
\max\nu_{(i)}<\max\bar{\nu}_{(i)}
.

The
(\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)})_{i}
define a partition of
\mathscr{T}^{>}([{\mathbf{r}}])
(the set of ordered trees on
[1,{\mathbf{r}}]
).

Figure 11.
An example of construction of the representation
(\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)})_{i}
from a clustering graph. The graph

We performed the following change of variables:

\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,{\mathbf{r}}-1\},\penalty\ \hat{x}_{i}:=x_{\min\nu_{(i)}}-x_{\min\bar{\nu}_{(i)}},\penalty\ \tilde{X}_{i}:=\tr_{-x_{\min\rho_{i}}}X_{\rho_{i}},

X_{2,n_{k+2}}\mapsto(\hat{x}_{1}\cdots,\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1},\tilde{X}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{X}_{{\mathbf{r}}}).

The condition
\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{q_{1},(s_{i})_{i}}
is integrated first with respect to
(\hat{x}_{1},\cdots,\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1})
, where relative positions inside a cluster
\tilde{X}_{i}
are kept constant. The
(\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}})_{i}
will be summed later with respect to the
(\tilde{X}_{i})_{i}
.

Fix
\tau_{i+1}
the time of the
(i+1)
-th clustering collision, and the relative positions
\hat{x}_{i-1},\cdots,\hat{x}_{1}
. We define the
i
-th clustering set

\begin{array}[]{cc}B_{i}:=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\omega_{2,\nu_{(i)}}\\
\bar{q}\in\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}\end{subarray}}B_{i}^{q,\bar{q}}\\
{\rm with}\qquad\omega_{1,\nu_{(i)}}:=\bigcup_{j\in\nu_{(i)}}\omega^{j}_{1},\qquad\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}:=\bigcup_{\bar{\jmath}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}\omega_{2}^{\bar{\jmath}},\\
B_{i}^{q,\bar{q}}:=\Big\{\hat{x}_{i}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \exists\tau_{i}\in[0,\tau_{i+1}\wedge T_{i}],\penalty\ |\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})|=\varepsilon\Big\}\end{array}

and
T_{i}:=2\theta
for the
({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}
first collisions,
t
else. We used that
\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2}
is the set of particles existing after time
\delta
.

Up to time
\tau_{i}
the curves
\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{q}
and
\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{\bar{q}}
are independent. Hence, we can perform the change of variables
\hat{x}_{i}\mapsto(\tau_{i},\eta_{i})
, where
\tau_{i}
is the minimal collision time and

\eta_{i}:=\frac{\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{q}(\tau_{i})}{|\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{{q}}(\tau_{i})|}.

The Jacobian of this diffeomorphism is
\varepsilon^{d-1}|(\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{{q}}(\tau_{i}))\cdot\eta_{i}|\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\eta_{i}
.

As the particles in
\omega_{1,\nu_{(i)}}
and
\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}
are isolated during
[\delta,\tau_{i}]
, their energies are conserved. The sum of relative velocities can be bounded by

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}\\
\bar{q}\in\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}\end{subarray}}|\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{{q}}(\tau_{i})|&\leq|\bar{{\mathsf{V}}}_{\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\tau_{i})|\,|\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}|^{1/2}|\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}|+|\bar{{\mathsf{V}}}_{\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\tau_{i})|\,|\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}|^{1/2}|\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}|\\[-15.0pt]
&\leq\Big(|\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}|+|\bar{{\mathsf{V}}}_{\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\tau_{i})|^{2}\Big)\Big(|\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}|+|\bar{{\mathsf{V}}}_{\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\tau_{i})|^{2}\Big).\end{split}

Using the same method as in the proof of
5.5
,

\frac{1}{2}\left|\bar{{\mathsf{V}}}_{\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\tau_{i})\right|^{2}\leq\mathscr{H}_{|\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}|}\left(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\delta)\right)\leq\mathscr{H}_{|\lambda_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}|}\left(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\lambda_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\delta)\right),

where we denote

\lambda_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}:=\bigcup_{\bar{\jmath}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}\lambda_{1}^{j}.

At time
\delta
, the particles in two different clusters cannot interact (by definition of a possible cluster),

\mathscr{H}_{|\lambda_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}|}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\lambda_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}}(\delta))=\sum_{j\in\nu_{(i)}}\mathscr{H}_{|\lambda_{1}^{j}|}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\lambda_{1}^{j}}(\delta))=\sum_{j\in\nu_{(i)}}\mathscr{H}_{|\lambda_{1}^{j}|}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\lambda_{1}^{j}}(0))\leq\sum_{j\in\nu_{(i)}}\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{j}|}(Z_{\rho_{j}}).

We conclude that

(8.9)

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\omega_{1,{\nu}_{(i)}}\\
\bar{q}\in\omega_{1,\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}\end{subarray}}|\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{{q}}(\tau_{i})|&\leq 4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{i}\in\nu_{(i)}\\
\bar{\nu}_{i}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}\end{subarray}}\Big(|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big).\end{split}

This gives the following bound on
|B_{i}|
(using the Boltzmann-Grad scaling
\mu\mathfrak{d}\varepsilon^{d-1}=1
)

\begin{split}|B_{i}|&\leq\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\int^{t_{i+1}\wedge T_{i}}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}\sum_{q,\bar{q}}|\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{v}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{i}\in\nu_{(i)}\\
\bar{\nu}_{i}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}\end{subarray}}\Big(|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\int^{t_{i+1}\wedge T_{i}}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}.\end{split}

Permuting the product and the sum,

\sum_{(\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)})_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{i}\in\nu_{(i)}\\
\bar{\nu}_{i}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}\end{subarray}}\Big(|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\\[-15.0pt]
=\sum_{(\nu_{i},\bar{\nu}_{i})_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\Big(|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big).

Using that

\forall a,b\in\mathbb{N},\penalty\ \frac{(a+b)!}{a!b!}\leq 2^{a+b},

we have

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}t_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\cdots\int_{0}^{t_{2}\wedge T_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}&\leq\frac{t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}}{(n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1)!}\frac{\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}}{(({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+})!}\leq 2^{n_{k+2}}\frac{t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}}{({\mathbf{r}}-1)!}.\end{split}

We can now sum up on the clustering collisions:

\begin{split}\int&\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i}}}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{1}\cdots\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\leq\sum_{(\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)})}\int\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{1}}\int\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2}}\cdots\int\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\int_{0}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}t_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\cdots\int_{0}^{t_{2}\wedge T_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\sum_{(\nu_{i},\bar{\nu}_{i})_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\Big(|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\\
&\leq\left(\frac{2C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\frac{t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}}{({\mathbf{r}}-1)!}\sum_{(\nu_{i},\bar{\nu}_{i})_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\Big(|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big).\end{split}

We can forget the order of the edges of
T^{>}=(\nu_{i},\bar{\nu}_{i})_{i}
, which gives a factor
{\mathbf{r}}!
. Secondly, denoting
d_{i}(G)
the degree of the vertex
i
in a graph
G
and
\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{r}}])
the set of minimally (not oriented) connected graphs on
[1,{\mathbf{r}}]
, we can write the preceding inequality as

\begin{split}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{q_{1},(s_{i})_{i}}}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{1}\cdots\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\leq\left(\frac{2C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{r}}])}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\Big(|\rho_{i}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})\Big)^{d_{i}(T)}.\end{split}

For
A,B>0
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\left(A+x\right)^{B}e^{-\frac{x}{4}}\leq\left(\tfrac{4B}{e}\right)^{B}e^{\frac{A}{4}}.

We use this inequality and that on
\mathscr{D}^{\underline{\rho}}_{\rho}
,
\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})=\sum_{i}\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})
to bound

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{1}\cdots\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\\
\begin{split}&\leq\left(\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}t^{n_{k}\wedge r-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{r}}])}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\Big(|\rho_{i}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})\Big)^{d_{i}(T)}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})}\\
&\leq\tilde{C}^{n_{k+2}}\frac{t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}}\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{r}}])}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}d_{i}(T)^{d_{i}(T)}\;.\end{split}

As the sum of the
d_{i}(T)
is equal to
2{\mathbf{r}}-2
, we have by convexity of
x\mapsto x\log x

\sum_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}d_{i}(T)\log{d_{i}(T)}\leq{\mathbf{r}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}d_{i}(T)}{{\mathbf{r}}}\log\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}d_{i}(T)}{{\mathbf{r}}}\leq(2{\mathbf{r}}-2)\log 2

and
|\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{r}}])|
is equal to
{\mathbf{r}}^{{\mathbf{r}}-2}
,

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{1}\cdots\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1}\\
\begin{split}&\leq\left(\frac{C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\right)^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}t^{n_{k}\wedge r-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{r}}])}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\Big(|\rho_{i}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})\Big)^{d_{i}(T)}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})}\\
&\leq\tilde{C}^{\prime n_{k+2}}\frac{t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}}({\mathbf{r}}-1)!\;.\end{split}

We can now integrate the condition
\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}})
. The particles in
Z_{\rho_{i}}
have to form a possible cluster. Because clusters are of size at most
\gamma
,

(8.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{|\rho_{1}|-1}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{|\rho_{1}|}}\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(Z_{\rho_{1}})\tfrac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{1}|}(Z_{\rho_{1}})}}{(2\pi)^{d|\rho_{1}|/2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{X}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\rho_{1}}\leq\frac{C_{r}\delta^{\max\{2,|\rho_{1}|-1\}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{|\rho_{1}|-1}}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}},

(8.11)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{|\rho_{i}|-1}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{|\rho_{i}|}}\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}})\tfrac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{i}|}(Z_{\rho_{i}})}}{(2\pi)^{d|\rho_{i}|/2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{X}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\rho_{i}}\leq C_{\gamma}\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathfrak{d}\mu}\right)^{|\rho_{i}|-1},

The second inequality is a clustering estimation, similar to the ones threatened in the proof of (
5.2
). In the first inequality, we use recollision estimates as in the proof of (
7.2
). The proofs are given in Appendix
B.4
.

Integrating the
\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
leads to

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i}}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}}}{(2\pi)^{dn_{k+2}/2}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k+2}}\\
\leq({\mathbf{r}}-1)!C^{n_{k+2}}\frac{t^{n_{k}\wedge{\mathbf{r}}-1}\theta^{({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{{\mathbf{r}}-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathfrak{d}\mu}\right)^{|\rho_{i}|-1}\frac{\delta^{\max{2,|\rho_{1}|-1}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{|\rho_{1}|-1}}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}.

Any particle removed at time
\delta
has a clustering collision during
[0,\delta]
. Therefore,
\sum_{i=1}^{\mathbf{r}}(|\rho_{i}|-1)
is bigger than
n_{k+2}-n_{k}
. In addition, we have chosen
\theta
bigger than
\delta
so

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i}}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{n_{k+2}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{dn_{k+2}}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k+2}}\\
\leq({\mathbf{r}}-1)!\frac{C^{n_{k+2}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}t^{n_{k}-1}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}.

We sum now on the parameters
(s_{i})_{i}
and
(\mathfrak{p}_{i})
. Because the sizes of the possible clusters are bounded by
\gamma
, the
|\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})|
are smaller than some
C_{\gamma}>0
depending only on
\gamma
. The number of collision parameters
(s_{i})_{i}
is equal to
2^{n_{k+2}}
and

\int\big|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{n_{k+2}})\big|M^{\otimes n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,n_{k+2}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k+2}}\\
\leq\frac{\|h\|(CC_{\gamma}4\gamma)^{n_{k+2}}}{n_{k+2}!(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}t^{n_{k}-1}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\sum_{r=1}^{n_{k+2}}\sum_{\underline{\rho}\in\mathscr{P}^{r}_{n_{k+2}}}({\mathbf{r}}-1)!\;.

The last sums can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{k+2}!}\sum_{r=1}^{n_{k+2}}\sum_{\underline{\rho}\in\mathscr{P}^{r}_{n_{k+2}}}({\mathbf{r}}-1)!

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{k+2}!}\sum_{{\mathbf{r}}=1}^{n_{k+2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\cdots+k_{\mathbf{r}}=n_{k+2}\\
k_{i}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{n_{k+2}!}{k_{1}!\cdots k_{\mathbf{r}}!}\frac{({\mathbf{r}}-1)!}{{\mathbf{r}}!}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\mathbf{r}}=1}^{n_{k+2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}+\cdots+k_{\mathbf{r}}=n_{k+2}\\
k_{i}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{k_{1}!\cdots k_{\mathbf{r}}!}\leq e^{n_{k+2}}

This ends the proof of the first inequality.
∎

Proof of (
8.3
).

As the
\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}
are symmetric, it is sufficient to study

(8.12)

\big|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{[n_{k+2}]})\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{[n_{k+2}+1-m,2n_{k+2}-m]})\big|.

The bound (
8.7
) leads to

\eqref{eq:PhiPhi}\leq\frac{\|h\|^{2}}{(n_{k+2}!)^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(q_{1},\omega_{1}\omega_{2})\\
(q_{1}^{\prime}\omega^{\prime}_{1}\omega^{\prime}_{2})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\varpi\subset[n_{k+2}]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s_{i})_{i}\\
(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})\\
(\lambda^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\lambda^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{l}}})\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t_{s}-\delta}_{\{q_{1}\},(s_{i})_{i}}}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1}}(\delta))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{0,t_{s}-\delta}_{\{q_{1}\},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}}(\bar{{\mathsf{Z^{\prime}}}}_{\omega^{\prime}_{2}}(\delta))\\[0.0pt]
\times\Bigg(\sum_{T\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{l}}])}\prod_{(i,j)\in E(T)}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lambda}_{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}{\lambda}_{j}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda_{1}|}^{[n_{k+1}]}(Z_{\lambda_{1}})\prod_{i=2}^{{\mathbf{l}}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda_{i}|}(Z_{\lambda_{i}})\Bigg)\\
\times\Bigg(\sum_{T^{\prime}\in\mathscr{T}([{\mathbf{l}}^{\prime}])}\prod_{(i,j)\in E(T^{\prime})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\lambda^{\prime}}_{i}\overset{\circ}{\sim}{\lambda^{\prime}}_{j}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda^{\prime}_{1}|}^{[n_{k+1}]}(Z_{\lambda^{\prime}_{1}})\prod_{i=2}^{{\mathbf{l}}}\Delta\hskip-6.0pt\Delta_{|\lambda^{\prime}_{i}|}(Z_{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}})\Bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{n}(k^{\prime}\delta)=n_{k}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}.

where we have denoted
\mathscr{T}(\sigma)
the set of connected and simply connected graphs with vertices
\sigma
, a finite set. The sets
\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}
have been defined in Definition
8.1
. In addition, we sum on all parameters such that

•

q_{1}\in[n_{k+2}]
and
q^{\prime}_{1}\in[n_{k+2}+1-m,2{n_{k+2}}-m]
,

•

\omega_{1}\sqcup\omega_{2}=[n_{k+2}]
,
\omega^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup\omega^{\prime}_{2}=[n_{k+2}+1-m,2{n_{k+2}}-m]
,
q_{1}\in\omega_{1}
,
q_{1}\in\omega_{1}
and
|\omega_{1}|=|\omega^{\prime}_{1}|=n_{k+1}
,

•

\lambda_{1}\supset\omega_{1}
,
\lambda^{\prime}_{1}\supset\omega^{\prime}_{1}
,
(\lambda_{2},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})
an unordered partition of
[n_{k+2}]\setminus\omega_{1}
and
(\lambda^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,\lambda^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{l}}})
an unordered partition of
[n_{k+2}+1-m,2{n_{k+2}}-m]\setminus\omega^{\prime}_{1}
.

The pseudotrajectory
\bar{Z}(\tau)
(respectively
\bar{Z}^{\prime}(\tau)
) begins with coordinates
Z_{[n_{k+2}]}
(respectively with coordinates
Z_{[n_{k+2}+1-m,2n_{k+2}-m]}
) with parameters
(q_{1},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}))
(respectively
(q^{\prime}_{1},\omega^{\prime}_{1},\omega^{\prime}_{2},(\lambda^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,

\lambda^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{l}}^{\prime}}))
) in the same way than in the proof of (
8.2
).

Note that the right hand-side of (
8.12
) is invariant under translation. Thus, one can fix
x_{1}=0
and integrate with respect to the other variables.

For a position
Z_{2n_{k+2}-m}
, we consider
\underline{\rho}:=(\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{{\mathbf{r}}})
the possible clusters. As in the previous section, with a little loss of symmetry, one can suppose that
\varpi_{1}\subset\rho_{1}
. We can then construct the parameters
\mathfrak{p}_{1}:=(\underline{\omega}^{1},\underline{\omega}^{\prime}{{}^{1}},\underline{\lambda}^{1},\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}{{}^{1}},\varpi)
,
\left(\mathfrak{p}_{i}\right)_{i\geq 2}:=\left((\underline{\omega}^{i},\underline{\omega}^{\prime}{{}^{i}},\underline{\lambda}^{i},\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}{{}^{i}})\right)_{i\geq 2}
:

•

\underline{\omega}^{i}:=(\omega^{i}_{1},\omega^{i}_{2})
is a partition of
\rho_{i}\cap[n_{k+2}]
defined by
\omega^{i}_{j}:=\omega_{j}\cap\rho_{i}
,

•

\underline{\omega}^{\prime}{{}^{i}}:=({\omega^{\prime}}^{i}_{1},{\omega^{\prime}}^{i}_{3})
is a partition of
\rho_{i}\cap[n_{k+2}+1-m,2n_{k+2}+m]
defined by
{\omega_{j}^{\prime}}^{i}:=\omega^{\prime}_{j}\cap\rho_{i}
,

•

\underline{\lambda}^{i}:=\{\lambda^{i}_{1}:=\lambda_{1}\cap\rho_{i}\}\cup\{\lambda_{j}\text{\penalty\ for\penalty\ }j\geq 2\text{\penalty\ with\penalty\ }\lambda_{j}\subset\rho_{i}\}
a partition of
[n_{k+2}]\cap\rho_{i}
,

•

\underline{\lambda^{\prime}}^{i}:=\{{\lambda^{\prime}_{1}}^{i}:=\lambda^{\prime}_{1}\cap\rho_{i}\}\cup\{\lambda^{\prime}_{j}\text{\penalty\ for\penalty\ }j\geq 2\text{\penalty\ with\penalty\ }\lambda^{\prime}_{j}\subset\rho_{i}\}
a partition of
[n_{k+2}+1-m,2n_{k+2}+m]\cap\rho_{i}
.

We denote now
\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})
the new set of possible parameters
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
(this will not create a conflict of notations with the previous section). Because each cluster
\rho_{i}
is of size at most
\gamma
,
|\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})|
is bounded by some constant
C_{\gamma}
depending only on
\gamma
. Defining

\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(Z_{\rho_{1}}):=\mathbbm{1}_{\!\!\!\!\tiny\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{\rho_{1}}\text{\,form\,a}\\
\text{possible\,cluster}\end{array}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall i\geq 2,\penalty\ \Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}}):=\mathbbm{1}_{\!\!\!\!\tiny\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{\rho_{i}}\text{\,form\,a}\\
\text{possible\,cluster}\end{array}}

\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}:=\Big\{Z_{2n_{k+2}-m}\in\mathscr{D}^{\underline{\rho}}_{\varepsilon},\,\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}_{\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2}\cup\omega_{3}}(\delta)\in\mathscr{R}^{0,k\theta+(k-1)\delta}_{\omega_{1},(s_{i})_{i}},\bar{{\mathsf{Z}}}^{\prime}_{n_{k+2}}(\delta)\in\mathscr{R}^{0,k\theta+(k-1)\delta}_{\omega^{\prime}_{1},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}\Big\},

we have as in the inequality (
8.8
)

\eqref{eq:PhiPhi}\leq\frac{\gamma^{2(\gamma-2)n_{k+2}}\|h\|_{0}^{2}}{(n_{k+2}!)^{2}}\sum_{{\mathbf{r}}=1}^{2n_{k+2}-m}\sum_{\underline{\rho}\in\mathscr{P}^{r}_{p}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s_{i})_{i},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}\\[1.0pt]
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}\in\underset{i}{\prod}\mathfrak{P}(\rho_{i})\end{subarray}}\penalty\ \mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}}(Z_{2n_{k+2}-m})\prod_{i=1}^{{\mathbf{r}}}\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}(Z_{\rho_{i}}).

Note that, for at least one
i
,
\underline{\varpi}^{i}
is not empty. We are now constructing a clustering tree in order to estimate
\mathscr{R}^{\underline{\rho},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}}_{(s_{i})_{i},(s^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}
.

Consider the collision graph associated with the first pseudotrajectory
\mathscr{G}^{[0,t-t_{s}]}_{\omega_{1}\cup\omega_{2}}
and the graph associated with the second one
\mathscr{G}^{[0,t-t_{s}]}_{\omega^{\prime}_{1}\cup\omega^{\prime}_{2}}
. Merge them and identify vertices in the same cluster
\rho_{i}
. Keeping only the first clustering collisions, we obtain the oriented tree
T^{>}:=(\nu_{i},\bar{\nu}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq{\mathbf{r}}-1}
. Note that these clustering collisions can happen in the first or second pseudotrajectories.

As in the proof of (
8.2
) we have to bound the number of collisions of
T^{>}
in the time interval
[0,2\tau]
. There are at most
(n_{k}-1+n_{k+2}-m)
collisions during
[(k^{\prime}+1)\delta,t-t_{s}]
(
n_{k}-1
for the first pseudotrajectory, and we have to connect
n_{k+2}-m
particles in the second). Thus, there are at least
({\mathbf{r}}-(n_{k}-1+n_{k+2}-m))_{+}
clustering collisions in
[\delta,(k^{\prime}+1)\delta]\subset[0,2\tau]
.

We explain quickly how to estimate the
i
-th collision. As in the previous paragraph, we construct the modified tree parameters
(\nu_{(i)},\bar{\nu}_{(i)})
and the change of variables

\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,{\mathbf{r}}-1\},\penalty\ \hat{x}_{i}:=x_{\min\nu_{(i)}}-x_{\min\bar{\nu}_{(i)}},\penalty\ \tilde{X}_{i}:=\tr_{x_{\min\rho_{i}}}X_{\rho_{i}},

X_{2,2n_{k+2}-m}\mapsto(\hat{x}_{1}\cdots,\hat{x}_{{\mathbf{r}}-1},\tilde{X}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{X}_{r}),

and we integrate on the
(\hat{x}_{i})
.

The clustering set
B_{i}
is defined as follows: fix
t_{i+1}
the time of the
(i+1)
-th clustering collision, and the relative positions
\hat{x}_{i-1},\cdots,\hat{x}_{1}
. We define the
i
-th clustering set

B_{i}:=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}q\in\bigcup_{j\in\nu_{(i)}}\rho_{j}\\
\overline{q}\in\bigcup_{\bar{\jmath}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}}\rho_{\bar{\jmath}}\end{subarray}}\Big(B_{i}^{q,\bar{q}}\cup B_{i}^{\prime q,\bar{q}}\Big)

with

B_{i}^{q,\bar{q}}:=\Big\{\hat{x}_{i}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \exists\tau_{i}\in[0,t_{i+1}\wedge T_{i}],\penalty\ |\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{\bar{q}}(\tau_{i})-\bar{{\mathsf{x}}}_{{q}}(\tau_{i})|=\varepsilon\Big\},

where
T_{i}:=2\theta
for the
({\mathbf{r}}-n_{k})_{+}
first collisions, and
t
else. The set
B_{i}^{\prime q,q^{\prime}}
is defined in the same way for the other pseudotrajectory. We can apply the estimates of the previous paragraph:

\int\mathbbm{1}_{B_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{x}_{i}\leq\frac{2C}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{i}\in\nu_{(i)}\\
\bar{\nu}_{i}\in\bar{\nu}_{(i)}\end{subarray}}\Big(|\rho_{\nu_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\nu_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\nu_{i}}})\Big)\Big(|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|+\mathscr{H}_{|\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}|}(Z_{\rho_{\bar{\nu}_{i}}})\Big)\int_{0}^{\tau_{i+1}\wedge T_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{i}.

In this way, we end up with the same situation as in the estimate of (
8.2
), and we can apply the same strategy:

\begin{split}\int|\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(Z_{k+2})\bar{\Phi}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}^{k^{\prime}}(&Z_{n_{k+2}+1-m,2n_{k+2}-m})\big|\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{2_{k+2}-m}(Z_{2n_{k+2}-m})}}{(2\pi)^{2n_{k+2}-m}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,2n_{k+2}-m}\,\mathrm{d}V_{2n_{k+2}-m}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{(2n_{k+2}-m)!\|h\|^{2}}{(n_{k+2}!)^{2}(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{2n_{k+2}-m-1}}C^{n_{k+2}}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\tau^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}t^{n_{k}-1+n_{k+2}-m}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{\mu^{m-1}}{n_{k+2}^{m}}\left(\frac{\|h\|}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k+2}-1}}\tilde{C}^{n_{k+2}}\right)^{2}\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{a}}\theta^{(n_{k+2}-n_{k}-2)_{+}}t^{n_{k}-1+n_{k+2}}\end{split}

which concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
The linearized Boltzmann operator without cut-off

In this section, we fix the dimension
d=3
. We construct the linearized Boltzmann operator associated with the power law
1/r^{s}
,
s>1
and we explain where the scaling
\mathfrak{d}_{s,\alpha}=\alpha^{2/s}
comes from.

We begin with a change of variables in the definition of the Boltzmann operator
\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}
. For
(v,v_{*},\nu)
, we define

(A.1)

\vec{\rho}:=\tfrac{\nu\wedge(v-v_{*})}{|v-v_{*}|}\in{\rm span}(v-v_{*})^{\perp}

the
impact parameters
, with the Jacobian

((v-v_{*})\cdot\nu)_{+}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\to|v-v_{*}|\,\mathrm{d}\vec{\rho}.

This allows us to redefine the post-collisional velocities
(v^{\prime},v_{*})
for an interaction potential
\mathscr{U}

(A.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}(v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime}):=\lim_{t\to\infty}(v_{a}(t),v_{b}(t))\\
\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(x_{a},x_{b})=(v_{a},v_{b}),\penalty\ \frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(v_{a},v_{b})=\alpha(-\nabla\mathscr{U}(x_{b}-x_{a}),\nabla\mathscr{U}(x_{b}-x_{a}))\\[5.0pt]
\lim_{t\to-\infty}(v_{a}(t),v_{b}(t))=:(v,v_{*}),\penalty\ (v_{a}-v_{b})\times(x_{a}-x_{b})=|v-v_{*}|\vec{\rho}.\end{array}\right.

With this definition, the scattering map can easily be defined for a not compactly supported decreasing potential.

For
\lambda>0
, we make the change of coordinate

(t,x_{a},x_{b},v_{a},v_{b})\mapsto(\tilde{t},\tilde{x}_{a},\tilde{x}_{b},v_{a},v_{b}):=(\lambda t,\lambda x_{a},\lambda x_{b},v_{a},v_{b})

In the new coordinates, the equations of motion become

\tfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{t}}(\tilde{x}_{a},\tilde{x}_{b})=(\tilde{v}_{a},\tilde{v}_{b}),\penalty\ \tfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{t}}(\tilde{v}_{a},\tilde{v}_{b})=\alpha\left(-\nabla\mathscr{U}\left(\tfrac{\tilde{x}_{a}-\tilde{x}_{b}}{\lambda}\right),\nabla\mathscr{U}\left(\tfrac{\tilde{x}_{a}-\tilde{x}_{b}}{\lambda}\right)\right)

Hence, the post-collisional parameters associated with
(v,v_{*},\vec{\rho})
and potential
\mathscr{U}
are the same as the ones associated with
(v,v_{*},\lambda\vec{\rho})
and potential
\mathscr{U}(\cdot/\lambda)
.

Performing the change of variable
\vec{\rho}\to\alpha^{-1/s}\vec{\rho}
in the collisional operator gives

\alpha^{2/s}\mathscr{L}_{\alpha\mathscr{V}}g=\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{U}_{\alpha}}g,\penalty\ {\rm where}\penalty\ \mathscr{U}_{\alpha}(r):=\alpha\mathscr{V}(r\alpha^{1/s})=\frac{f\left(r\alpha^{\frac{1}{s}}\right)}{r^{s}}.

This new potential converges when
\alpha\to 0
to
\mathscr{U}^{s}(r):=1/r^{s}
. It is natural to guess the convergence of the operators

\frac{1}{\alpha^{2/s}}\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}\to\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{U}^{s}}.

Section 3 of
[
LBW22
]
is dedicated to the proof of this claim.

Appendix B
Geometrical estimates

B.1.
Estimation the length scattering time

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\mathscr{V}
an interaction potential that is radial and supported in a ball of diameter
\varepsilon
.

We consider two particles,
1
and
2
, with initial coordinates

(x_{a}(0),v_{a}(0))=(0,v_{1}),\penalty\ (x_{b}(0),v_{b}(0))=(\varepsilon\nu,v_{2}),\penalty\ \nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\penalty\ (v_{1}-v_{2})\cdot\nu<0,

following the Hamiltonian dynamics linked to

H:=\tfrac{|v_{a}|^{2}+|v_{b}|^{2}}{2}+\mathscr{V}(\tfrac{x_{a}-x_{b}}{\varepsilon}).

Then, the scattering time is bound by

(B.1)

[\tau]:=\inf\{\tau>0\penalty\ |x_{a}(\tau)-x_{b}(\tau)|>\varepsilon\}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon|v_{1}-v_{2}|}{|\nu\times(v_{1}-v_{2})|^{2}}.

Proof.

The motion equation are written as

\begin{split}\tfrac{d}{dt}\left(x_{a}+x_{b}\right)=\left({v}_{a}+v_{b}\right)&\quad\tfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(v_{a}+v_{b}\right)=0\\
\tfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(x_{a}-x_{b}\right)=\left(v_{a}-v_{b}\right)&\quad\tfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(v_{a}-v_{b}\right)=-\tfrac{2}{\varepsilon}\nabla\mathscr{V}\left(\tfrac{x_{a}-x_{b}}{\varepsilon}\right).\end{split}

Hence,
[\tau]
does not depend on
v_{1}+v_{2}
.

We use the impact parameter
\rho:=\tfrac{|\varepsilon\nu\times(v_{1}-v_{2})|}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|}
defined in (
A.1
). The time
[\tau]
is given by (see chapter 8 of
[
GSRT13
]
)

(B.2)

\displaystyle[\tau]=\frac{2}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|}\int_{r_{\min}}^{\varepsilon/2}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{\sqrt{1-\tfrac{\rho^{2}}{r^{2}}-2\frac{\mathscr{V}(r/\varepsilon)}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{2}}}},

with
r_{\min}
defined by

1-\tfrac{\rho^{2}}{r_{\min}^{2}}-2\frac{\mathscr{V}(r_{\min}/\varepsilon)}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{2}}=0.

We begin by performing the change of variables

(B.3)

u^{2}:=\frac{\rho^{2}}{r^{2}}+2\frac{\mathscr{V}(r/\varepsilon)}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{2}}

which implies

(B.4)

\displaystyle[\tau]=\frac{2}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|}\int_{\rho/\varepsilon}^{1}\frac{u\,\mathrm{d}u}{\sqrt{1-u^{2}}}\frac{r}{\frac{\rho^{2}}{r^{2}}-\frac{r\mathscr{V}^{\prime}(r/\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon|v_{1}-v_{2}|^{2}}}

Using that
\mathscr{V}^{\prime}
is non positive and that
u\geq\tfrac{\rho}{r}
,
r\leq\varepsilon

[\tau]\leq\frac{2}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|}\int_{\rho/\varepsilon}^{1}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}u}{\sqrt{1-u^{2}}}\frac{u\varepsilon^{3}}{\rho^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{|v_{1}-v_{2}|\rho^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon|v_{1}-v_{2}|}{|\nu\times(v_{1}-v_{2})|^{2}}.

∎

B.2.
Proof of Proposition
7.2

The goal of this section is proving the following estimations (
6.8
) and (
7.4
):

Lemma B.2
.

Let
\mathscr{V}
be an interaction potential that is radial, decreasing, and supported in a ball of radius
\varepsilon
.

There exists a
C>0
independent of
\mathscr{V}
such that for
t=n_{k}\theta

(B.5)

\sum_{T=(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i},s_{i})_{i\leq n_{k}-1}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{n_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\left(1-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\right)\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}{\nu}_{[n_{k}-1]}\,\mathrm{d}{\tau_{i}}M^{\otimes n_{k}}dV_{n_{k}}\\
+\sum_{\bar{T}}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k+2}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{>,t-t_{s},\bar{T}}_{\{q\},\omega,(s_{i})_{i}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k+2}-1}|({\rm v}^{\varepsilon}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\rm v}^{\varepsilon}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}(\tau_{i}))\cdot\nu_{i}|\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tau_{i}}M^{\otimes n_{k+2}}dV_{n_{k+2}}\\
\leq C^{n_{K}}(n_{k})^{n_{k}}\theta^{(n_{k}-n_{k-1}-2)_{+}}t^{n_{k}}\varepsilon^{1/5}.

The two estimations can be performed in the same way, and we will only treat the first one. The proof of this lemma is an adaptation of the proof of Lemma 8 of
[
PSS14
]
. The estimation is not optimal. For example, the factor
\varepsilon^{1/4}
can be replaced in the hard spheres setting by
\varepsilon|\log\varepsilon|^{r}
for some constant
r>1
(see for example
[
BGSRS23b
]
). However, optimal estimates use the upper bound of the collision kernel of hard spheres. Such bounds are verified for more general potential and certainly not in the limit
\alpha\to 0
. The proof of
[
PSS14
]
(which is adapted from it) is more robust.

Proof.

We need to avoid

•

an
overlap
: there exists a time
\tau\in(0,t)\cap\delta\mathbb{Z}
and two particles
q
and
q^{\prime}
such that

\left|{\mathsf{x}}_{q}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)\right|\leq\varepsilon,

•

a
recollision
: there exists a time
\tau\in[0,t]
and two particles
q
and
q^{\prime}
such that
\tau\notin\{\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{n_{k}-1}\}
and

\left|{\mathsf{x}}_{q}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)\right|=\varepsilon\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ \left({\mathsf{x}}_{q}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)\right)\cdot\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q}(\tau)-{\mathsf{v}}_{{q^{\prime}}}(\tau)\right)<0.

We begin with the estimation of overlap, which is easier.

As the
i
-th collision between particles
(q_{i},\bar{q_{i}})
can last only a time
\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\times\nu_{i}\right)\right|^{2}}
(see (
B.1
)), there can be an overlap only if there is some
\tau\in\delta\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,t]
such that
\tau_{i}
is in the interval

I_{\varepsilon}(\tau,V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}):=\left[\tau-\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\times\nu_{i}\right)\right|^{2}},\tau\right]

Hence, the set of parameters leading to an overlap is smaller than

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in\delta\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,t]\\
1\leq i\leq n_{k}-1\end{subarray}}\sum_{T}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{n_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{\varepsilon}(\tau,V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})}(\tau_{i})\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tau_{[n_{k}-1]}}M^{\otimes n_{k}}dV_{n_{k}}\\
\leq\tfrac{C^{n_{k}}t^{1+n_{k-1}}\theta^{(n_{k}-n_{k-1}-1)_{+}}}{\delta n_{k}^{n_{k}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n_{k}-1\\[3.0pt]
T\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}t\wedge\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{k}-1]})\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}}M^{\otimes n_{k}}dV_{n_{k}}

We can apply the same estimates as in Lemma
6.1
and for the terms (
6.11
). We conclude that the set of overlap has a measure smaller than

\frac{\varepsilon^{1/2}C^{n_{k}}t^{n_{k-1}+1}\theta^{(n_{k}-n_{k-1}-1)_{+}}}{\delta n_{k}^{n_{k}}}.

We treat the recollision now.

We denote
\mathscr{G}
the collision graph, and we define
\mathscr{G}^{[0,\tau)}
the subgraph of
\mathscr{G}
with edges

\left\{(q,\bar{q})_{\tau^{\prime},\sigma}\in E(G),\penalty\ \tau^{\prime}<\tau\right\}.

If the first recollision involves particles
q
and
q^{\prime}
at time
\tau_{\rm rec}
, we consider
\omega\subset[n_{k+2}]
the connected components of
\{q,q^{\prime}\}
in the collision graph
\mathscr{G}^{[0,\tau_{\rm rec})}
. Before the time
\tau_{\rm rec}
, the pseudotrajectory
{\mathsf{Z}}_{\omega}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)
and its formal limit
{\mathsf{Z}}_{\omega}(\tau)
are close up to a translation, and using Lemma
6.3
, there exists a
y_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
such that

\forall\tau\in[0,\tau_{\rm rec}],\penalty\ |{\mathsf{X}}^{0}_{\omega}(\tau)-\tr_{y_{0}}{\mathsf{X}}^{\varepsilon}_{\omega}(\tau)|\leq 2n_{K}\mathbb{V}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k+2}-1}1\wedge\tfrac{\varepsilon\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}.

Hence, there is a recollision if at time
\tau_{\rm rec}\in[0,t]\setminus\bigcup_{i}\{\tau_{i}\}
,

(B.6)

\exists\penalty\ q,q^{\prime}\in[n_{k}-1]\penalty\ {\rm such\penalty\ that}\penalty\ |{\mathsf{x}}^{0}_{q}(\tau_{\rm rec})-{\mathsf{x}}^{0}_{q^{\prime}}(\tau_{\rm rec})|\leq\varepsilon+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k+2}-1}1\wedge\varepsilon\tfrac{2n_{K}\mathbb{V}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}.

We can study only the limiting flow and defining a contact as "there exists a time
\tau_{\rm rec}
such that (
B.6
) is verified": we have

\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}\setminus\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{\varepsilon}_{T}\subset\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}\cap\{\text{at\penalty\ least\penalty\ one\penalty\ contact}\}.

The first step is to forbid the collisions that last too long. We define
\mathscr{E}_{1}\subset\mathfrak{T}_{\underline{n}_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}
as (for some
c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,1)
)

\mathscr{E}_{1}:=\bigg\{\forall i\leq n_{k}-1,\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|\min\left\{1,(\tau_{i}-\tau_{i-1}),(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i})\right\}\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}}\\
\tfrac{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}{\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}\min\left\{1,(\tau_{i}-\tau_{i-1}),(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i})\right\}\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{2}}}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}}\bigg\}.

In
\mathscr{E}_{1}
there is a contact if there exists a time
\tau_{\rm rec}
such that

(B.7)

|{\mathsf{x}}^{0}_{q}(\tau_{\rm rec})-{\mathsf{x}}^{0}_{q^{\prime}}(\tau_{\rm rec})|\leq 3\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}},

and

1-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{E}_{1}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}-1}\mathbbm{1}_{\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\frac{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}{\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{2}}}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}}}\\
+\mathbbm{1}_{|\tau_{i}-\tau_{i-1}|\leq\max\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|},\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{2}}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\right)}\\
+\mathbbm{1}_{|\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}|\leq\max\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|},\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{2}}\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}{n_{k}^{2}\mathbb{V}\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}\right)}.

Now, we place ourselves in
\mathscr{E}_{1}
and we fix a collision tree
T
. The first contact happens at time
\tau_{\rm rec}
between particles
q_{\rm rec}
and
q^{\prime}_{\rm rec}
. There exists a collision
i_{0}
, two disjoint sequences of collisions
(i_{j})_{j\leq p}
and
(i^{\prime}_{j})_{j\leq p^{\prime}}
and two sequences of particles
(a_{j})_{j\leq p}
,
(a^{\prime}_{j})_{j\leq p^{\prime}}
such that

•

\forall j\geq 1
,
i_{0}<i_{j}
and
i_{0}<i^{\prime}_{j}
,

•

\forall j\geq 1
,
a_{j}\in\{q_{i_{j}},q^{\prime}_{i_{j}}\}\cap\{q_{i_{j-1}},q^{\prime}_{i_{j-1}}\}
and
a^{\prime}_{j}\in\{q_{i^{\prime}_{j}},q^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}_{j}}\}\cap\{q_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}},q^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}}\}
,

•

if for
j<j^{\prime}
,
a_{j}=a_{j^{\prime}}
. Then for any
i\in[i_{j},i_{j^{\prime}}]
such that
a_{j}\in\{q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i}\}
, we have
j\in\{i_{j},i_{j+1},\cdots,i_{j^{\prime}}\}
, and similarly for the sequences
(i^{\prime}_{j})_{j\leq p^{\prime}}
,
(a^{\prime}_{j})_{j\leq p^{\prime}}
,

•

a_{0}=q_{i_{0}}
,
a^{\prime}_{0}=q^{\prime}_{i_{0}}
and
\{a_{p},a^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}\}=\{q_{\rm rec},q^{\prime}_{\rm rec}\}
.

There is a contact if

(B.8)

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[-t,t]{R}\\
y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\left|y_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}{\mathsf{v}}_{a_{j}}(]\tau_{i_{j-1}},\tau_{i_{j}}[)(\tau_{i_{j}}-\tau_{i_{j-1}})+{\mathsf{v}}_{a_{p}}(]\tau_{i_{p}},\tau_{\rm rec}[)(s-\tau_{i_{p}})\right.\\
\left.-\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}(]\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}},\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}[)(\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}-\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}})+{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}}(]\tau_{i^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}},\tau_{\rm rec}[)(s-\tau_{i^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}})\right|\leq\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}.

In addition, if the
(\tau_{i})_{i}
and
(\tau^{\prime}_{i})_{i}
verify
\tau_{i_{0}}<\tau_{i_{1}}<\cdots<\tau_{i_{p}}<\tau_{\rm rec}
and
\tau_{i_{0}}<\tau_{i^{\prime}_{1}}<\cdots<\tau_{i^{\prime}_{p}}<\tau_{\rm rec}
, there is a contact if

(B.9)

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[\tau_{i_{0}+1},t]{R}\\
y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\left|y_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}{\mathsf{v}}_{a_{j}}(]\tau_{i_{j-1}},\tau_{i_{j}}[)(\tau_{i_{j}}-\tau_{i_{j-1}})+{\mathsf{v}}_{a_{p}}(]\tau_{i_{p}},\tau_{\rm rec}[)(s-\tau_{i_{p}})\right.\\
\left.-\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}(]\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}},\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}[)(\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}-\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}})+{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}}(]\tau_{i^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}},\tau_{\rm rec}[)(s-\tau_{i^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}})\right|\leq\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}.

Remark B.1
.

Note that we can perform the previous construction if
\tau_{\rm rec}>\tau_{i_{0}}
. If we study the overlap for particles of size
\varepsilon
, it is always the case. But for punctual particles, the pathology can happen before the first collision. Our proof can be adapted by taking
i_{0}
such that
i_{0}>i_{j}
and
i_{0}>i^{\prime})_{j}
, making the change of variable
V_{n_{k}}\mapsto{\mathsf{V}}_{n_{k}}(\tau_{i_{0}}^{-})
and looking at everything backwards.

Denoting

\displaystyle\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}:={\mathsf{v}}_{a_{j+1}}(]\tau_{i_{j}},\tau_{i_{j+1}}[)-{\mathsf{v}}_{a_{j}}(]\tau_{i_{j-1}},\tau_{i_{j}}[),

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Delta{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{j}:={\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{j+1}}(]\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}},\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j+1}}[)-{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}(]\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j-1}},\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}[),

\displaystyle\penalty\ w_{0}:={\mathsf{v}}_{a_{0}}(\tau_{i_{0}}^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{0}}(\tau_{i_{0}}^{+}),\penalty\ \text{and}

\displaystyle\penalty\ w_{\rm f}={\mathsf{v}}_{a_{p}}(]\tau_{i_{p}},\tau_{\rm rec}[)-{\mathsf{v}}_{a^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}}(]\tau_{i_{p^{\prime}}^{\prime}},\tau_{\rm rec}[),

the equation (
B.8
) can be written as

(B.10)

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in[-t,t]\\
y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\left|y_{0}-\tau_{i_{0}}w_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\tau_{i_{j}}-\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}^{\prime}\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}+sw_{\rm f}\right|\leq\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}

In the following, we denote
\hat{w}_{\rm f}:=\tfrac{{w}_{\rm f}}{|{w}_{\rm f}|}
. We take the cross product with
\hat{w}_{\rm f}
in (
B.10
):

(B.11)

\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\left|(y_{0}-\tau_{i_{0}}w_{0})\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}\tau_{i_{j}}-\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{j}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}\tau_{i^{\prime}_{j}}\right|\leq\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}.

We fix
y_{0}
such that the minimum is reached. We distinguish three cases:

(1)

If there exists a
\ell_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{1}}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}|\geq\tfrac{\varepsilon^{c_{3}}}{n_{k}}
(for some
c_{3}\in(c_{1},1)
),
\tau_{i_{\ell_{1}}}
has to be in an interval of length
\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}-c_{3}}
.

(2)

Else, if
y_{0}
is non zero, using that

w_{0}=-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}^{\prime}-w_{\rm f}

we have

|w_{0}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}\left|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}\right|\leq\varepsilon^{c_{3}}.

Hence
\left|y_{0}\times w_{f}\right|
has to be smaller than
2t\mathbb{V}\varepsilon^{c_{3}}+\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}
. Combining with (
B.11
), we deduce that there exists a collision
j
, a vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
|y_{0}|\leq n_{K}t\mathbb{V}
, and a couple of particles
(q,q^{\prime})
such that
w_{\rm f}=\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}}(\tau_{j}^{-})\right)
, and then

\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}}(\tau_{j}^{-})\right)\times y_{0}\right|\leq 2t\mathbb{V}\varepsilon^{c_{3}}+\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}.

(3)

We treat now the case
y_{0}=0
. We begin to show that there exists some
\ell^{\prime}
such that
|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell^{\prime}}|
or
|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}|
is bigger than
\tfrac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{3n_{k}t}
(in the following, we suppose that it is
|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell^{\prime}}|
).

Proof.

We proceed by contradiction. As for all
j\leq p
, we are in one of the three following cases,

(a)

i_{j-1},i_{j+1}
are smaller than
i_{j}
, and

\left|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\right|=\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i_{j}}}(\tau_{i_{j}}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{i_{j}}}(\tau_{i_{j}}^{-})\right|,

(b)

i_{j-1},i_{j+1}
are bigger than
i_{j}
, and

\left|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\right|=\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i_{j}}}(\tau_{i_{j}}^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{i_{j}}}(\tau_{i_{j}}^{+})\right|,

(c)

i_{j-1}<i_{j}<i_{j+1}
or
i_{j-1}<i_{j}<i_{j+1}
.

As the difference of velocities is conserved by the scattering map and we are in
\mathscr{E}_{1}
, the cases (a) and (b) are impossible. Using that
i_{0}<i_{1}
, we deduce that
\tau_{i_{0}}<\tau_{i_{1}}<\cdots<\tau_{i_{p}}<\tau_{\rm rec}
. In the same way,
\tau_{i^{\prime}_{0}}<\tau_{i^{\prime}_{1}}<\cdots<\tau_{i^{\prime}_{p^{\prime}}}<\tau_{\rm rec}
.

Using triangular inequality,

|w_{f}-w_{0}|=\left|\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}\Delta{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{j}\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{3t}.

Considering the
s\in[\tau_{i_{0}+1},t]
such that the minimum of (
B.11
) is reached, we have

\displaystyle|(\tau_{i_{0}+1}-\tau_{i_{0}})w_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq|(\tau_{i_{0}}-s)w_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{j}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{p^{\prime}}\left|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}^{\prime}_{j}\right|\right)t+s|w_{f}-w_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}+\frac{2\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{3},

which is impossible since we are in
\mathscr{E}_{1}
.
∎

As
|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}|\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon^{c_{3}}}{n_{k}}
, (else it would have been treated in Point (1))

\left|\frac{\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{0}}}{|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{0}}|}\times\hat{w}_{\rm f}\right|\leq\frac{t\varepsilon^{c_{3}}}{n_{k}|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{0}}|}\leq t\varepsilon^{c_{3}-c_{1}}\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ \left|w_{0}\times\hat{w}_{f}\right|\leq\varepsilon^{c_{3}}.

Finally, we obtain

(B.12)

\left|\frac{\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{0}}}{|\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{0}}|}\times{w}_{0}\right|\leq\varepsilon^{c_{3}}+t\varepsilon^{c_{3}-c_{1}}.

Definition B.1
.

For
\omega\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we denote (using
\xi_{\alpha}
defined in Definition
2.1
)

(\tfrac{w^{\prime}}{2},-\tfrac{w^{\prime}}{2},\nu^{\prime})=\xi_{\alpha}((w/2,-w/2,\nu)).

We define

(B.13)

\zeta_{1}(w,\nu):=w,\penalty\ \zeta_{2}(w,\nu):=w^{\prime},\penalty\ \zeta_{3}(w,\nu):=\frac{w-w^{\prime}}{2}\penalty\ \text{and}\penalty\ \zeta_{4}(w,\nu):=\frac{w+w^{\prime}}{2}.

Note that
\Delta{\mathsf{v}}_{\ell_{0}}
is equal one of the
(\pm\zeta_{i}({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{\ell_{0}}}(\tau_{\ell_{0}}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{\ell_{0}}}(\tau_{\ell_{0}}^{-}),\nu_{\ell_{0}})_{i\leq 4}
.

We conclude that

(B.14)

\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{n_{k}}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tau_{n_{k}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\rm contact}\leq\frac{C^{n_{k}}t^{n_{k-1}}\theta^{(n_{k}-n_{k-1}-1)_{+}}}{n_{k}^{n_{k}}}\Bigg(\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}-c_{3}}+\\
+\sum_{i}\mathbbm{1}_{\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|\leq\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}\tfrac{\varepsilon^{c_{1}}}{\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau^{-}_{i})\right|}+\mathbbm{1}_{\frac{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right)\times\nu_{i}\right|^{2}}{\left|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{\bar{q}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{-})\right|}\leq\varepsilon^{c_{2}}}\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\\
|y_{0}|\leq tn_{k}\mathbb{V}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\leq n_{k}\\
(q,q^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{\left|\left({\mathsf{v}}_{q}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}}(\tau_{j}^{-})\right)\times y_{0}\right|\leq 2t\mathbb{V}\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}}\\
+\sum_{i,j}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}\mathbbm{1}_{\left|\frac{\zeta_{\ell}({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-}),\nu_{j})}{|\zeta_{\ell}({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-}),\nu_{j})|}\times({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+}))\right|\leq t\varepsilon^{c_{3}-c_{1}}}\Bigg).

We have to integrate now with respect to
(\nu_{[n_{k}-1]},V_{n_{k}})
. As in the proof of (
6.10
), we use the applications
\Xi^{i}_{T}
defined by (
6.12
).

We recall that

\Xi_{T}^{i+1}\Xi_{T}^{i}\cdots\Xi_{T}^{1}(\nu_{[n_{k}-1]},V_{n_{k}})=(\tilde{\nu}_{[n_{k}-1]},\tilde{V}_{n_{k}}={\mathsf{V}}_{n_{k}}(\tau_{i}^{+}))

and that the Jacobian of this application is
1
. We can integrate with respect to
{\mathsf{v}}_{q}(\tau^{-}_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q}(\tau^{-}_{i})
.

We treat only the last singularity; the other ones can be estimated in the same way. If we remove the edges
(q_{j},q^{\prime}_{j})
from
T
, either
q_{j}
or
q_{j}^{\prime}
is not in the connected component of
\{q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i}\}
. Without loss of generality, we suppose that it is
q_{j}
. We denote
\omega
the connected components of
q_{j}
in
T\setminus\{(q_{j},q^{\prime}_{j},s_{j})\}
. Before the collision
j
, the particles of
\omega
are independent of the other ones, and as before, we can construct an application of Jacobian
1

\bar{\Xi}:(\nu_{[n_{k}-1]},V_{n_{k}})\mapsto(\bar{\nu},{\mathsf{V}}_{\omega^{c}})(\tau_{i}^{+}),{\mathsf{V}}_{\omega}(\tau_{j}^{-})).

In addition,

\begin{split}|V_{n_{k}}|^{2}&=\frac{|{\mathsf{V}}_{n_{k}}(\tau_{i}^{+})|^{2}}{2}+\frac{|{\mathsf{V}}_{n_{k}}(\tau_{j}^{-})|^{2}}{2}\\
&\geq\frac{|{\mathsf{V}}_{\omega^{c}}(\tau_{i}^{+})|^{2}}{2}+\frac{|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})|^{2}+|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})|^{2}}{2}+\frac{|{\mathsf{V}}_{\omega\setminus\{q_{j}\}}(\tau_{j}^{-})|^{2}}{2}\\
&\geq\frac{|{\mathsf{V}}_{\omega^{c}}(\tau_{i}^{+})|^{2}}{2}+\frac{|{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})|^{2}}{4}+\frac{|{\mathsf{V}}_{\omega\setminus\{q_{j}\}}(\tau_{j}^{-})|^{2}}{2},\end{split}

and denoting
w:={\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})
, we can integrate with respect to the velocities

\displaystyle\sum_{T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\mathbbm{1}_{\left|\frac{\zeta_{\ell}({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-}),\nu_{j})}{|\zeta_{\ell}({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}(\tau_{j}^{-}),\nu_{j})|}\times({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}(\tau_{i}^{+}))\right|\leq\varepsilon^{1/4}}\Lambda_{T}(V_{n_{K}},\nu_{[n_{K}-1]})M^{\otimes n_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{n_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbbm{1}_{\left|\frac{\zeta_{\ell}(w,\nu_{j})}{|\zeta_{\ell}(w,\nu_{j})|}\times({v}_{q_{i}}-{v}_{q^{\prime}_{i}})\right|\leq\varepsilon^{1/4}}\frac{(C(1+|V_{n_{k}}|^{2}))^{n_{k}}e^{-\frac{|V_{[n_{k}]\setminus\{q_{j}\}}|^{2}}{4}-\frac{|w|^{2}}{8}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{n_{k}d}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{[n_{k}-1]}}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{V}_{[n_{k}]}\,\mathrm{d}{w}

\displaystyle\leq n_{k}^{2n_{k}}C^{n_{k}}\varepsilon^{c_{3}-c_{1}}.

We have first integrate with respect to
(v_{q_{i}},c_{q_{i}^{\prime}})
and then the
(w,\nu_{j})
. Finally, we obtain

(B.15)

\frac{1}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}-1}}\sum_{T}\int_{\mathfrak{T}_{n_{k}}\times\mathfrak{G}^{0}_{T}}\mathbbm{1}_{\rm reco}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{k}-1}|({\mathsf{v}}_{q_{i}}(\tau_{i})-{\mathsf{v}}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}(\tau_{i}))\cdot\nu_{i}|\,\mathrm{d}{\nu_{i}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tau_{i}}M^{\otimes n_{k}}dV_{n_{k}}\\
\leq\frac{(n_{k}C)^{n_{k}}t^{n_{k-1}}\theta^{(n_{k}-n_{k-1}-1)_{+}}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{n_{k}-1}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{c_{2}}{2}}+\varepsilon^{c_{3}-c_{1}}+\varepsilon^{c_{1}}+\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}}+\varepsilon^{1-c_{2}-c_{3}}\right).

We choose
c_{1}=\tfrac{1}{5}
and
c_{2}=c_{3}=\tfrac{2}{5}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

B.3.
Proof of (
3.6
)

Lemma B.3
.

For
r\leq 2\gamma
,

(B.16)

\int\mathbbm{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z_{r}\text{\,form\,a\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ }\\
{\rm possible\,cluster}\end{subarray}}M^{\otimes r}\,\mathrm{d}Z_{r}\leq C_{\gamma}\mu^{-r+1}\delta^{r-1}.

Proof.

First choose a family
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{p}
of subset covering
[r]
, and
(\underline{\lambda}_{i})_{i\leq p}=(\lambda_{i}^{1},\cdots,\lambda_{i}^{l_{i}})_{i\leq p}
a family of partitions of
\omega_{i}
. As
n
is bounded, there are a finite number of possible
((\omega_{i})_{i},(\underline{\lambda}_{i})_{i})
. We construct the graph
\mathscr{G}
as the merge of the collision graph of
{\mathsf{Z}}(\tau,Z_{\omega_{i}},\underline{\lambda}_{i})
on
[0,\delta]
, and we extract
T
the clustering tree (there are less than
(2\gamma)^{2\gamma}
possible clustering trees). We can then adapt the proof of (
5.3
) (where we treated only two pseudotrajectories), and we obtain the expected result.
∎

B.4.
Proof of (
8.10
)

We recall that
\mathscr{O}_{r}\subset\mathbb{D}^{r}
is the set

\mathscr{O}_{r}:=\Big\{Z_{r}\in\mathbb{D}^{r},\penalty\ \exists(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}),\penalty\ \text{the\penalty\ collision\penalty\ graph\penalty\ of\penalty\ }{\mathsf{Z}}_{r}(\cdot,Z_{z},(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}}))\text{\penalty\ on\penalty\ }[0,\delta]\penalty\ \text{is\penalty\ }\\
\text{connected\penalty\ and\penalty\ the\penalty\ pseudotrajectory\penalty\ has\penalty\ a\penalty\ collision\penalty\ or\penalty\ a\penalty\ multiple\penalty\ interaction}\Big\}.

Proposition B.4
.

There exists a positive constant
C_{r}
depending only on the dimension and the number of particles
r
such that

(B.17)

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{r-1}\times B_{r}(\mathbb{V})}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{r}}(Z_{r})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{r}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{rd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{r}\leq\frac{C_{r}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{r-1}}\delta^{2}\theta^{r-2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{1/12}},

where
B_{r}(\mathbb{V})
is the ball of radius
\mathbb{V}
in dimension
rd
(we use that
\delta=\varepsilon^{1/12}
).

Proof.

For
r=2
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{D}^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}(Z_{2})\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{2}}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\,\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{2}=C\varepsilon^{d}\leq\frac{C\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\mu\mathfrak{d}}

as
\delta^{2}\varepsilon^{1/12}=\varepsilon^{3/12}\leq\varepsilon
.

Fix parameters
(\lambda_{1},\cdots\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})
and denote
\mathscr{R}_{(\lambda_{1},\cdots\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})}\subset\mathbb{D}^{r}
the set of initial configuration such that the pseudotrajectory has a connected collision graph and a pathological collision.

As we suppose that the collision graph is connected, we can construct a clustering tree
T:=(q_{i},\bar{q}_{i})_{i\leq r-1}
as in the proof of Proposition
5.2
. We define
\tau_{\rm path}
the time of the first pathological collision. The corresponding collision can either create a loop in the collision graph or be a clustering multiple encounter
.

The first case can be treated as a recollision, which is already done in the proof of Proposition
5.2
and in the preceding section, we have

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{r-1}\times B_{r}(\mathbb{V})}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{(\lambda_{1},\cdots\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})}^{\rm reco}}\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{r}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{rd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{r}\leq\frac{C_{r}\delta^{r-2}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{r-1}}\varepsilon^{1/5}\leq\frac{C_{r}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{r-1}}\delta^{2}\theta^{r-2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{1/12}}.

In the second case, there are two clustering collisions
j<\tilde{\jmath}
such that
\{q_{j},\bar{q}_{j}\}\cap\{q_{\tilde{\jmath}},\bar{q}_{\tilde{\jmath}}\}
and

\forall\tau\in(\tau_{j},\tau_{\tilde{\jmath}}),\penalty\ |{\mathsf{x}}_{q_{j}}(\tau)-{\mathsf{x}}_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau)|<\varepsilon.

Two particles
(q_{j},q^{\prime}_{j})
can stay at a distance shorter than
\varepsilon
on a time interval shorter than

1\wedge\frac{\varepsilon|v_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j})-v_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j})|}{|(v_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j})-v_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j}))\wedge(x_{q_{j}}(\tau_{j})-x_{\bar{q}_{j}}(\tau_{j}))|^{2}}

(whose integral is bounded by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
). Hence, using the same strategy than in the proof of Proposition
5.2
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{r-1}\times B_{r}(\mathbb{V})}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{(\lambda_{1},\cdots\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})}^{\rm mult}}\frac{e^{-\mathscr{H}_{r}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{rd}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}X_{2,r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{r}\leq\frac{C_{r}\delta^{r-1}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{r-2}}\varepsilon^{1/2}\leq\frac{C_{r}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{r-1}}\delta^{2}\theta^{r-2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{1/12}}.

Summing on all the possible
(\lambda_{1},\cdots\lambda_{{\mathbf{l}}})
, we obtain the expected result.
∎

Proof of (
8.10
).

We have now to prove Estimation (
8.10
):

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{r-1}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}\mathbbm{1}_{\!\!\!\!\tiny\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{r}\text{\,form\,a}\\
\text{possible\,cluster}\end{array}}\tfrac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{r}(Z_{r})}}{(2\pi)^{dr/2}}\,\mathrm{d}{X}_{2,r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{r}\leq\frac{C_{r}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{r-1}}\delta^{2}\theta^{r-2}\varepsilon^{\mathfrak{1/12}},

Without loss of generality, we suppose that
1\in\varpi
.

Fix the family
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{p}
of subset covering
[n]
,
and
(\underline{\lambda}_{i})_{i\leq p}=(\lambda_{i}^{1},\cdots,\lambda_{i}^{l_{i}})_{i\leq p}
a family of partition of
\omega_{i}
such that the union of the collision graph associated to parameters
((\lambda_{i}^{j})_{j})_{i}
is connected.

We begin by fix
Z_{\varpi}
and sum the clustering of the particle in
[n]\setminus\varpi

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{n-1}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}\mathbbm{1}_{\!\!\!\!\tiny\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{r}\text{\,form\,a}\\
\text{possible\,cluster}\end{array}}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{n}(Z_{n})}}{(2\pi)^{dn/2}}\,\mathrm{d}{X}_{\varpi}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\varpi}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\mathscr{O}_{\varpi}}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{H}_{|\varpi|}(Z_{\varpi})}}{(2\pi)^{d|\varpi|/2}}\frac{C_{n}\delta^{n-|\varpi|}}{(\mu\mathfrak{d})^{|\varpi|}}.

Then, we integrate with respect to
\,\mathrm{d}{X}_{\varpi\setminus\{1\}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\varpi}
.
∎
